tubes, and each of these tubes can be filled with about
(a^{\prime}/b)^{4}
essentially distinct
b\times b\times 1
tubes.

By the definition of
D
, we have

D\geq\frac{|P|}{|P^{{\dagger}}|}\big(\#\mathcal{P}[P^{{\dagger}}]\big)^{1/2},

i.e.
\#\mathcal{P}[P^{{\dagger}}]\leq\big(D\frac{|P^{{\dagger}}|}{|P|}\big)^{2}.

Thus by (
5.26
),

\#\mathcal{P}[\hat{P}]\lesssim\Big(\frac{|\hat{P}|}{|P^{\dagger}|}\Big)^{2}\#\mathcal{P}[P^{{\dagger}}]\lesssim\Big(\frac{|\hat{P}|}{|P^{\dagger}|}\Big)^{2}\Big(D\frac{|P^{{\dagger}}|}{|P|}\Big)^{2},

which is (
5.25
).
∎

5.3
Proof of Proposition
5.1
: Tubes that factor through flat boxes

With Proposition
5.14
in hand, we are now ready to prove Proposition
5.1
. Fix
\omega>0,\ 0<\sigma\leq 2/3
, and suppose
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega)
is true (and thus by Proposition
5.14
,
\mathcal{F}(\sigma,\omega)
is true). Let
\kappa,\eta>0
be small quantities to be specified below. Let
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
be
\delta^{\eta}
dense, let
\delta\leq a\leq b\leq 1
, and let
\mathcal{W}
be a set of congruent copies of an
a\times b\times 2
prism
W_{0}
, as described in the statement of Proposition
5.1
.

Step 1.

After dyadic pigeonholing, we can find a refinement
(\mathbb{T}_{1},Y_{1})_{\delta}
of
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
with
\sum_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}}|Y_{1}(T)|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\sum_{T\in\mathbb{T}}|Y(T)|
, and a subset
\mathcal{W}_{1}\subset\mathcal{W}
so that for each
W\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
and each
x\in\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}[W]}Y_{1}(T)
, we have

\Big|B(x,a)\cap\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|\sim\lambda|B(x,a)|,\quad\textrm{with}\quad\lambda\geq|W|^{-1}\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}[W]}Y_{1}(T)\Big|,

(5.27)

where the “density”
\lambda=\lambda(W)
is the same (up to a factor of 2) for all
W\in\mathcal{W}
. In words, (
5.27
) says that if we blur the shading
\bigcup_{\mathbb{T}_{1}[W]}Y_{1}(T)
at scale
a
(for example by convolving with the characteristic function of
B(0,a)
), then each point in the shading has density
\sim\lambda
.

After further pigeonholing, we can ensure that each set
(\mathbb{T}_{1}[W],Y_{1})_{\delta},\ W\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
is
\approx_{\delta}\delta^{O(\eta)}
dense;
\mathcal{W}_{1}
is a
\approx_{\delta}\delta^{-O(\eta)}
balanced cover of
\mathbb{T}_{1}
; and each set
\mathbb{T}_{1}^{W}
obeys the Frostman Convex Wolff axioms with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-O(\eta)}
. Abusing notation, we will continue to refer to the output of this pigeonholing by
(\mathbb{T}_{1},Y_{1})_{\delta}
and
\mathcal{W}_{1}
.

Step 2.
Fix
W\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
. Then
(\mathbb{T}_{1}^{W},Y_{1}^{W})_{\delta/b\times\delta/a\times 1}
is a collection of convex sets, each comparable to a
\delta/b\times\delta/a\times 1
prism, with a
\delta^{O(\eta)}
-dense shading. We first consider the case where
b\geq\delta^{1-\varepsilon/100}
, so
\delta/b\leq\delta^{\varepsilon/100}
. In this case, the shading on
(\mathbb{T}_{1}^{W},Y_{1}^{W})_{\delta/b\times\delta/a\times 1}
is
(\delta/b)^{O(\eta)/\varepsilon}
dense. If
\eta
is selected sufficiently small, then we can apply the estimate
\mathcal{F}(\sigma,\omega)
with
\varepsilon/2
in place of
\varepsilon
(recall that
\mathcal{F}(\sigma,\omega)
is true in light of Proposition
5.14
) to conclude that

\Big|\bigcup_{T^{W}\in\mathbb{T}_{1}^{W}}Y_{1}^{W}(T^{W})\Big|\geq\kappa_{\varepsilon}\big(\frac{\delta}{b}\big)^{\varepsilon/2}\big(\frac{\delta}{a}\big)^{\omega}m_{W}^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T}^{W})|T^{W}|\Big(m_{W}^{-3/2}\ell_{W}(\#\mathbb{T}^{W})|T^{W}|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma},

(5.28)

where
m_{W}=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{W})
and
\ell_{W}=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T}^{W})\leq C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{W})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-O(\eta)}
. Recall as well that
|T^{W}|\sim|T|/|W|
. Note that the estimate
\mathcal{F}(\sigma,\omega)
involves the additional term “
D
” defined in (
5.6
), but as discussed in Remark
5.6
, Situation 3, we have
D\lesssim 1
.

Since
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{W})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-O(\eta)}
, we have that
m_{W}\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-O(\eta)}|T^{W}|(\#\mathbb{T}^{W})
, and thus (
5.28
) allows us to estimate the density
\lambda
(recall that
\lambda
was defined in (
5.27
)):

\lambda\geq\Big|\bigcup_{T^{W}\in\mathbb{T}^{W}}Y^{W}(T^{W})\Big|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\kappa_{\varepsilon}\big(\frac{\delta}{b}\big)^{\varepsilon/2}\delta^{O(\eta)}\big(\frac{\delta}{a}\big)^{\omega}\Big((\#\mathbb{T}^{W})^{1/2}|T^{W}|\Big)^{\sigma}.

(5.29)

Note that Inequality (
5.29
) is currently only valid when
b\geq\delta^{1-\varepsilon/100}
. Next we consider the case where
b<\delta^{1-\varepsilon/100}
, so each
T^{W}\in\mathbb{T}_{1}^{W}
has dimensions comparable to
d_{1}\times d_{2}\times 1
for some
1\geq d_{2}\geq d_{1}\geq\delta^{\varepsilon/100}
. This is true because

\lambda\geq|Y^{W}(T^{W})|\geq\delta^{O(\eta)}|T^{W}|\geq\delta^{\varepsilon/50+O(\eta)}\geq\delta^{\varepsilon/10}.

Step 3.
For each
W\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
, define the shading

\tilde{Y}_{1}(W)=W\cap N_{a}\Big(\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}[W]}Y_{1}(T)\Big).

By (
5.27
), we have

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|\gtrsim\lambda\Big|\bigcup_{W\in\mathcal{W}_{1}}\tilde{Y}_{1}(W)\Big|.

(5.30)

Our next task is to show that
(\mathcal{W}_{1},\tilde{Y}_{1})_{a\times b\times 1}
is
\delta^{O(\eta)}
dense. The argument is shown in Figure
8
. Fix
W\in\mathcal{W}
. Since
(\mathbb{T}_{1}^{W},Y_{1}^{W})
is
\gtrapprox\delta^{O(\eta)}
dense, we can select a refinement so that each
T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}[W]
satisfies
|Y_{1}(T)|\gtrapprox\delta^{O(\eta)}|T|
. After pigeonholing, we can select a set
\mathbb{T}_{a}
of essentially distinct
a
-tubes that form a
\approx_{\delta}1
balanced cover of
\mathbb{T}_{1}[W]
. Since
(\mathbb{T}_{1}^{W},Y_{1}^{W})
satisfies the Frostman Convex Wolff axioms with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-O(\eta)}
, we have that
\mathbb{T}_{a}
satisfies the Frostman Convex Wolff axioms with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-O(\eta)}
. The shading
Y_{a}(T_{a})=T_{a}\cap N_{a}\big(\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}[W]}Y_{1}(T)\big)
is
\approx_{\delta}\delta^{O(\eta)}
dense. Applying Lemma
5.7
to
(\mathbb{T}_{a}^{W},Y_{a}^{W})_{a/b\times 1\times 1}
and then undoing the scaling
\phi_{W}
, we conclude that

\Big|W\cap N_{a}\Big(\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}[W]}Y_{1}(T)\Big)\Big|\geq\Big|\bigcup_{T_{a}\in\mathbb{T}_{a}}Y_{a}(T_{a})\Big|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{O(\eta)}|W|.

Figure 8:
Each
a
-tube in
\mathbb{T}_{a}
(blue tubes) contains at least one
\delta
-tube from
\mathbb{T}_{1}[W]
(red tube); this
\delta
-tube has a dense shading (red dots), which in turn gives us a dense shading on the
a
-tube that contains it (blue balls; for clarify we have only drawn these for one of the tubes in
\mathbb{T}_{a}
). Finally, since
W
(black box) has thickness
a
, and the
a
-tubes inside
W
satisfy a (rescaled) Frostman Convex Wolff axioms, an
L^{2}
argument says that the union of
a
-tubes has almost full volume.

Step 4.

It remains to estimate the RHS of (
5.30
).
We first consider the case where
a\leq\delta^{\varepsilon/100}
.
In this case,
(\mathcal{W}_{1},\tilde{Y}_{1})_{a\times b\times 1}
is
a^{O(\eta/\varepsilon)}
dense. If
\eta
is selected sufficiently small depending on
\varepsilon
, then we can apply the estimate
\mathcal{F}(\sigma,\omega)
to conclude that

\Big|\bigcup_{W\in\mathcal{W}_{1}}\tilde{Y}_{1}(W)\Big|\geq\kappa_{\varepsilon}a^{\varepsilon/2}b^{\omega}\tilde{m}^{-1}(\#\mathcal{W}_{1})|W|\Big(\tilde{m}^{-3/2}\tilde{\ell}(\#\mathcal{W}_{1})|W|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma},

(5.31)

where
\tilde{m}=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{W}_{1})
and
\tilde{\ell}=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathcal{W}_{1}).
Note that the estimate
\mathcal{F}(\sigma,\omega)
involves the additional term “
D
” defined in (
5.6
). However, we claim that both both Parts (A) and (B) of Proposition
5.1
, we have

D\lesssim\delta^{-\eta}.

(5.32)

We verify this claim as follows. In Part (A) of Proposition
5.1
, we have the hypothesis that
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{W})\leq\delta^{-\eta}
. As discussed in Remark
5.6
, Situation 1, this ensures that
D\lesssim\delta^{-\eta}
. In Part (B) of Proposition
5.1
, we have the hypothesis that

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{W}[N_{b}(W)])\leq\delta^{-\eta}\quad\textrm{for all}\ W\in\mathcal{W}.

As discussed in Remark
5.6
, Situation 2, this ensures that
D\lesssim\delta^{-\eta}
. This establishes (
5.32
).

In summary, we have established (
5.31
) when
a\leq\delta^{\varepsilon/100}
. If instead
a>\delta^{\varepsilon/100}
, then (
5.31
) follows from the estimate

\Big|\bigcup_{W\in\mathcal{W}_{1}}\tilde{Y}_{1}(W)\Big|\gtrsim\delta^{\varepsilon/20}\gtrsim\delta^{\varepsilon/20}\tilde{m}^{-1}(\#\mathcal{W}_{1})|W|.

(5.33)

Combining (
5.29
), (
5.30
), and (
5.31
) (when
a\leq\delta^{\varepsilon/100}
) or (
5.33
) (when
a\geq\delta^{\varepsilon/100}
), we conclude that

\begin{split}\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|&\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\omega+\varepsilon+O(\eta)-\varepsilon^{2}/200}\Big(\frac{b}{a}\Big)^{\omega}\tilde{m}^{-1}(\#\mathcal{W}_{1})|W|\Big(\tilde{m}^{-3/2}\tilde{\ell}(\#\mathcal{W}_{1})|W|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma}\Big(\Big(\frac{\#\mathbb{T}}{\#\mathcal{W}_{1}}\Big)^{1/2}\frac{|T|}{|W|}\Big)^{\sigma}\\
&=\delta^{\omega+\varepsilon-\varepsilon^{2}/300}\Big(\frac{b}{a}\Big)^{\omega}\tilde{m}^{-1}(\#\mathcal{W}_{1})|W|\Big(\tilde{m}^{-3/2}\tilde{\ell}(\#\mathcal{W}_{1})^{3/2}|W|^{3/2}(\#\mathbb{T})^{-1/2}|T|^{-1}\Big)^{-\sigma}.\end{split}

(5.34)

Step 5.

It remains to analyze the RHS of (
5.34
). Our analysis will differ for Parts (A) and (B) of Proposition
5.1
. We begin with Part (A). We have
\tilde{m}\lesssim\delta^{-\eta}
, and since
\mathcal{W}_{1}
is a
\approx_{\delta}\delta^{-O(\eta)}
balanced cover of
\mathbb{T}_{1}
, by Remark
4.3
(A) (i.e. Frostman Wolff constants are inherited upwards) we have
\delta^{\eta}\tilde{\ell}\lessapprox_{\delta}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T})=\ell
. Since
\mathcal{W}
is a
\delta^{-\eta}
-balanced,
\delta^{-\eta}
-almost partitioning cover of
\mathbb{T}
,

\#\mathbb{T}[W]\geq\delta^{\eta}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})\frac{|W|}{|T|}
for each
W\in\mathcal{W}
, and
\mathcal{W}_{1}
is a refinement of
\mathcal{W}
, we have

(\#\mathcal{W}_{1})|W|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\eta}m^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|
. The RHS of (
5.34
) becomes

\delta^{\omega+\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{b}{a}\Big)^{\omega}m^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|\Big(m^{-3/2}\ell(\#\mathbb{T})|T|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma},

as claimed.

Next we consider part (B). We have
\tilde{m}\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\eta}|W|(\#\mathcal{W})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\eta}|W|(\#\mathcal{W}_{1})
, and
\tilde{\ell}\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\eta}
. Thus the RHS of (
5.34
) becomes

\delta^{\omega+\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{b}{a}\Big)^{\omega}\Big((\#\mathbb{T})^{1/2}|T|\Big)^{\sigma}.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.1
.

5.4
Proof of Proposition
5.2
: Factoring at two scales

The proof of Proposition
5.2
is almost identical to the proof of Proposition
5.1
. Rather than present a more complicated unified proof of the two results, for clarity of exposition we have opted to instead briefly sketch the proof of Proposition
5.2
and highlight where the two proofs differ.

We begin by refining the shading
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
to find a subset
(\mathbb{T}_{1},Y_{1})_{\delta}
that has at least average density on balls of radius
a
; this is the analogue of (
5.27
). By Lemma
4.11
, we have that for each
W\in\mathcal{W}
,
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T}^{W})\lessapprox_{\delta}\lessapprox_{\delta}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T}^{T_{\rho}})\cdot C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\rho}^{W})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\eta}\ell^{\prime}
. Thus the analogue of (
5.28
) is

\Big|\bigcup_{T^{W}\in\mathbb{T}_{1}^{W}}Y_{1}^{W}(T^{W})\Big|\gtrsim\big(\frac{\delta}{b}\big)^{\varepsilon/2}\big(\frac{\delta}{a}\big)^{\omega}m^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T}^{W})|T^{W}|\Big(m^{-3/2}(\delta^{-\eta}\ell^{\prime})(\#\mathbb{T}^{W})|T^{W}|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma},

(5.35)

where
m=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})
, and this gives us the following analogue of (
5.29
):

\lambda\gtrapprox_{\delta}\big(\frac{\delta}{b}\big)^{\varepsilon/2}\delta^{O(\eta)}\big(\frac{\delta}{a}\big)^{\omega}m^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T}[W])|T|\Big(m^{-3/2}\ell^{\prime}(\#\mathbb{T}^{W})|T^{W}|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma}.

(5.36)

The next step is to define a dense shading on
\mathcal{W}
; it is here that we use the fact that
\mathcal{W}
factors
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
from below with respect to the Frostman Convex Wolff axioms with error
\leq\delta^{-\eta}
— this allows us to use the same argument as in Step 3 from the proof of Proposition
5.1
to show that the shading
\tilde{Y}_{1}(W)
is
\delta^{O(\eta)}
dense.

Finally, we have the following analogue of (
5.31
):

\Big|\bigcup_{W\in\mathcal{W}_{1}}\tilde{Y}_{1}(W)\Big|\gtrsim a^{\varepsilon/2}b^{\omega}\tilde{m}^{-1}(\#\mathcal{W}_{1})|W|\Big(\tilde{m}^{-3/2}\tilde{\ell}(\#\mathcal{W})|W|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma},

(5.37)

where
\tilde{m}=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{W}_{1})\leq\delta^{-\eta}
(by hypothesis) and
\tilde{\ell}=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathcal{W}_{1})\lessapprox\delta^{-\eta}\ell
(by Remark
4.3
(A) ).

Combining (
5.36
) and (
5.37
) (using the same argument that was used to obtain (
5.34
)), we conclude that

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|\gtrsim\delta^{\varepsilon+\omega}\big(\frac{b}{a}\big)^{\omega}m^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|\Big(m^{-3/2}\ell\ell^{\prime}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma}.

5.5
Tubes organized into to slabs

We conclude this section by using the tools developed thus far to prove the following. Let
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
be a set of tubes and their associated shading. Suppose that for each
T\in\mathbb{T}
, there is a
\delta\times b\times 1
slab
S\supset T
that has large intersection with
\bigcup_{\mathbb{T}}Y(T)
. Then provided
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})
is small,
\bigcup_{\mathbb{T}}Y(T)
has larger volume than one would expect from the estimate
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega)
; the estimate becomes better as
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})
becomes smaller and
b
becomes larger. The precise statement is as follows.

Lemma 5.17
.

Let
\omega>0,\sigma\in[0,2/3]
and suppose
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega)
is true. For all
\varepsilon>0
, there exist
\kappa,\eta>0
so that the following holds for all
\delta>0
. Let
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
be a set of tubes and their associated shading. Let
b\geq\delta
and suppose that for each
T\in\mathbb{T}
there exists a
\delta\times b\times 1
prism
S\supset T
with
\big|S\cap\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\big|\geq\delta^{\eta}|S|
. Then

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|\geq\kappa\delta^{\varepsilon}b^{\omega}m^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|\big(m^{-1}\ell(\#\mathbb{T})|T|^{1/2}\big)^{-\sigma}\Big(\frac{|S|}{|T|}\Big)^{\sigma/2},

(5.38)

where
m=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})
and
\ell=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T})
.

Remark 5.18
.

Note that the exponent of
m
in (
5.38
) is only
m^{\sigma-1}
, rather than the usual estimate
m^{(3/2)\sigma-1}
from
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega)
. While this is likely not optimal, in practice we will only apply Lemma
5.17
with
m
of size about 1, so the distinction will not be important.

Proof.

After pigeonholing, we can select a
\approx_{\delta}1
refinement
(\mathbb{T}_{1},Y_{1})_{\delta}
of
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
and a set
\mathcal{S}
of essentially distinct
\delta\times b\times 1
slabs with the following properties:

•

For each
T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}
with corresponding slab
S(T)
, there is a slab
S\in\mathcal{S}
comparable to
S(T)
. We denote this by
T\sim S

•

There is an integer
N
so that each slab in
\mathcal{S}
, there are between
N
and
2N
tubes
T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}
with
T\sim S
.

Abusing notation, we will replace each slab in
\mathcal{S}
with its 10-fold dilate. Then

(i)

\mathcal{S}
covers
\mathbb{T}_{1}
,

(ii)

\#\mathbb{T}[S]\geq N
for each
S\in\mathcal{S}
.

(iii)

\#\mathcal{S}\sim N^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T}_{1})
.

(iv)

The shading
Y(S)=S\cap\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)
is
\gtrsim\delta^{\eta}
dense.

From Items (ii) and (iii) we conclude that

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathcal{S})\lesssim C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T}_{1})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\eta}\ell,

and

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{S})\lesssim C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{1})\frac{\#\mathcal{S}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{1}}\frac{|S|}{|T|}\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\eta}m\frac{\#\mathcal{S}}{\#\mathbb{T}}\frac{|S|}{|T|}.

(5.39)

We would like to use Proposition
5.14
and apply the estimate
\mathcal{F}(\sigma,\omega)
to obtain a lower bound for the volume of
\bigcup Y(S)
. Before doing so, we should estimate the quantity
D
from (
5.6
). By Remark
5.6
, Situation 2, and (
5.39
), we have

D\lesssim\Big(\sup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{S}[N_{b}(S)])\Big)^{1/2}\lessapprox_{\delta}\Big(\delta^{-\eta}m\frac{\#\mathcal{S}}{\#\mathbb{T}}\frac{|S|}{|T|}\Big)^{1/2}.

If
\eta,\kappa>0
are chosen sufficiently small depending on
\omega,\sigma,
and
\varepsilon
, then we can apply the estimate
\mathcal{F}(\sigma,\omega)
to conclude that

\begin{split}\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|&\geq\Big|\bigcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}Y(S)\Big|\\
&\geq\kappa\delta^{\varepsilon/2}b^{\omega}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{S})^{-1}(\#\mathcal{S})|S|\Big(C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{S})^{-3/2}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathcal{S})(\#\mathcal{S})|S|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma}D^{-\sigma}\\
&\gtrapprox_{\delta}\kappa\delta^{\varepsilon/2+2\eta}b^{\omega}m^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|\Big(m^{-1}\ell(\#\mathbb{T})|T|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma}\Big(m^{1/2}\frac{|S|}{|T|}\frac{(\#\mathcal{S})^{1/2}}{(\#\mathbb{T})^{1/2}}D^{-1}\Big)^{\sigma}\\
&\gtrapprox_{\delta}\kappa\delta^{\varepsilon/2+2\eta}b^{\omega}m^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|\Big(m^{-1}\ell(\#\mathbb{T})|T|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma}\Big(\frac{|S|}{|T|}\Big)^{\sigma/2}.\qed\end{split}

Often, we will use the following weaker version of Lemma
5.17
.

Corollary 5.19
.

Let
\omega>0,\sigma\in(0,2/3]
and suppose
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega)
is true. For all
\varepsilon>0
, there exist
\eta,c>0
so that the following holds for all
\delta>0
. Let
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
be a set of tubes and their associated shading. Let
\rho\geq\delta
and suppose that for each
T\in\mathbb{T}
there exists a
\rho
tube
T_{\rho}\supset T
with
\big|T_{\rho}\cap\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\big|\geq\delta^{\eta}|T_{\rho}|
. Then

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|\geq\kappa\delta^{\varepsilon}\rho^{\omega}m^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|\big(m^{-1}\ell(\#\mathbb{T})|T|^{1/2}\big)^{-\sigma}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\delta}\Big)^{\sigma/2},

(5.40)

where
m=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})
and
\ell=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T})
.

6
Assertions
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{E}
are equivalent

Our goal in this section is to prove Proposition
1.6
. To do so, we will need a result from
[
26
]
, which (informally) says that if a set of
\delta
-tubes satisfies the Frostman Convex Wolff Axioms at many different scales, then the union of these tubes must have large volume. To state the result precisely, we recall Definition 2.12 from
[
26
]
.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
K\geq 1,\delta>0
. We say a set
\mathbb{T}
of
\delta
-tubes in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfies the
Frostman Convex Wolff Axioms at every scale with error
K
if the tubes in
\mathbb{T}
are essentially distinct, and for every
\rho_{0}\in[\delta,1]
, there exists
\rho\in[\rho_{0},K\rho_{0})
and a set of
\rho
-tubes
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
that satisfies the following properties.

(i)

\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
is a
K
-balanced partitioning cover of
\mathbb{T}
.

(ii)

For each
T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
,
\mathbb{T}^{T_{\rho}}
satisfies the Frostman Convex Wolff Axioms with error
K
.

Next we recall Theorem 5.2 from
[
26
]
.

Theorem 6.2
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\eta,\kappa>0
so that the following holds for all
\delta>0
. Let
\mathbb{T}
be a set of
\delta
-tubes that satisfy the Frostman Convex Wolff Axioms at every scale with error
\delta^{-\eta}
, and let
Y(T)
be a
\delta^{\eta}
dense shading. Then

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|\geq\kappa\delta^{\varepsilon}.

(6.1)

When
\varepsilon<\omega
, the conclusion of Theorem
6.2
gives a volume estimate that is superior to the volume estimate (
1.3
) from Assertion
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega)
. However, the hypotheses of Assertion
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega)
are weaker. The next result says that for every collection
\mathbb{T}
of tubes that satisfies the Frostman Convex Wolff axioms, at least one of the following must occur:

(A)

\mathbb{T}
satisfies the hypothesis of Theorem
6.2
. This is good, provided we select
\varepsilon<\omega
.

(B)

At a suitable scale,
\mathbb{T}
satisfies the Katz-Tao Convex Wolff axioms with error roughly 1. This is good, if we know that
\mathcal{D}(\sigma,\omega_{1})
is true for some
\omega_{1}<\omega.

(C)

At a suitable scale,
\mathbb{T}
is factored by flat rectangular prisms, and thus satisfies the hypotheses of Proposition
5.1
. This is good, since it gives a stronger volume estimate than
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega)
.

The precise statement is as follows.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
\zeta_{1}\geq\zeta_{2}\geq\zeta_{3}>0
. Then there exists
\eta>0
such that the following holds for all
\delta>0
. Let
\mathbb{T}
be a set of
\delta
-tubes satisfying the Frostman Convex Wolff Axioms with error
\leq\delta^{-\eta}
. Then after replacing
\mathbb{T}
by a
\approx_{\delta}1
refinement, at least one of the following is true.

(A)

\mathbb{T}
satisfies the Frostman Convex Wolff Axioms at every scale with error
\delta^{-\zeta_{1}},
in the sense of Definition
6.1
.

(B)

There exists
\delta\leq\tau<\rho\leq 1
, with
\tau\leq\delta^{\zeta_{1}/5}\rho;
a balanced partitioning cover
\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
of
\mathbb{T}
; and a balanced partitioning cover
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
of
\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
such that the following is true:

(i)

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{T_{\tau}})\lesssim\delta^{-\zeta_{2}}
for each
T_{\tau}\in\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
.

(ii)

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}})\lesssim\delta^{-\zeta_{2}}
and
\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}}\geq\delta^{\zeta_{2}}(\rho/\tau)^{2}
for each
T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
.

(iii)

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\rho})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\eta}.

(C)

There exists
\delta\leq a<b\leq 1
with
a\leq\delta^{\zeta_{2}/100}b
, and a set
\mathcal{W}
of
a\times b\times 1
prisms that satisfies the hypotheses of Proposition
5.1
(B):
\mathcal{W}
factors
\mathbb{T}
above and below with respect to the Frostman Convex Wolff Axioms, with error
O(\delta^{-\zeta_{3}})
. And for each
W\in\mathcal{W}
, we have
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{W}[N_{b}(W)])\lesssim\delta^{-\zeta_{3}}
.

We will defer the proof of Proposition
6.3
to Section
6.3
.

Using Proposition
6.3
, we will prove the following weaker form of Proposition
1.6
; recall that
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}
is defined in Definition
5.3
.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
0<\sigma\leq 2/3
. For all
\omega,t>0
, there exists
\alpha>0
so that the following holds for all
\omega^{\prime}\geq\omega+t
. Suppose
\mathcal{D}(\sigma,\omega)
and
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega^{\prime})
are true. Then
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}(\sigma,\omega^{\prime}-\alpha)
is true.

Proof.

Let
\alpha=\alpha(\sigma,\omega,t)>0
be a small number to be specified below. Let
\eta,\kappa>0
be small numbers that depend on
\omega,\omega^{\prime},
and
\sigma
. Our goal is to prove that if
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
is
\delta^{\eta}
dense with
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T})\leq\delta^{-\eta}
, then

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}\Big|\geq\kappa\delta^{\omega^{\prime}-\alpha}m^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|\Big(m^{-3/2}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma},

(6.2)

with
m=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})
. Note that the estimate (
6.2
) is slightly stronger than the desired estimate
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}(\sigma,\omega^{\prime}-\alpha)
, since there is no additional
\delta^{\varepsilon}
loss; this stronger estimate is possible since
\omega^{\prime}-\omega>0
, which gives us a bit of “wiggle room.”

Step 1.

Without loss of generality we may suppose that
|Y(T)|\geq\delta^{\eta}|T|
for each
T\in\mathbb{T}
. Let
\zeta_{i}=\zeta_{i}(\sigma,\omega,t)
,
i=1,\ldots,5
be small quantities to be chosen below; we will have
\zeta_{i+1}
very small compared to
\zeta_{i}
, and
\eta
very small compared to
\zeta_{5}
.

We first consider the case where there exists a subset
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{T}
with
\#\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\geq\delta^{\eta}(\#\mathbb{T})
, and
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\leq\delta^{-\zeta_{4}}
(this assumption will remain until Step 4, where we will consider the case where no such subset exists). Abusing notation slightly, we will continue to use
\mathbb{T}
to refer to this subset. In particular, we have that

\#\mathbb{T}\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\eta+\zeta_{4}}m|T|^{-1},\quad\textrm{and}\quad C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})\leq\delta^{-\zeta_{4}}.

(6.3)

If
\zeta_{4}
and
\eta
are chosen sufficiently small compared to
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\zeta_{3}
, then we can apply Proposition
6.3
to
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
, with
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\zeta_{3}
as specified above. We will select
\zeta_{1}=\zeta_{1}(\omega)
and
\eta
sufficiently small so that if Conclusion (A) of Proposition
6.3
holds, then we can use Theorem
6.2
to conclude that

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|\gtrsim\delta^{\omega},

and hence (
6.2
) holds provided we choose
\alpha(\omega,t)\leq t
. Henceforth we shall assume that Conclusion (A) of Proposition
6.3
does
not
hold.

Step 2.
Suppose that Conclusion (B) of Proposition
6.3
holds. We will define shadings
Y_{\tau}
and
Y_{\rho}
on the sets of tubes
\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
and
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
as follows. For each
T_{\tau}\in\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
, we refine the shading on
\mathbb{T}[T_{\tau}]
to have average density on balls of radius
\tau
(see (
5.27
) and the surrounding discussion). We define the shading
Y_{\tau}(T_{\tau})
to be the union of those
\tau
-balls that intersect
\bigcup_{\mathbb{T}[T_{\tau}]}Y(T)
. We perform the analogous procedure to define
Y_{\rho}
(this induces a refinement on the shadings
Y_{\tau}
and
Y
).

After these steps have been performed,
(\mathbb{T}_{\rho},Y_{\rho})_{\rho}
is
\delta^{O(\eta)}
dense; each pair
(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}},Y_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}})_{\tau/\rho}
is
\delta^{O(\eta)}
dense; and each pair
(\mathbb{T}^{T_{\tau}},Y^{T_{\tau}})_{\delta/\tau}
is
\delta^{O(\eta)}
dense. Furthermore, we have

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|\geq\bigg(\Big|\bigcup_{T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}}Y_{\rho}(T_{\rho})\Big|\bigg)\bigg(\inf_{T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}}\Big|\bigcup_{T_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}}\in\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}}}Y_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}}(T_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}})\Big|\bigg)\bigg(\inf_{T_{\tau}\in\mathbb{T}_{\tau}}\Big|\bigcup_{T^{T_{\tau}}\in\mathbb{T}^{T_{\tau}}}Y^{T_{\tau}}(T^{T_{\tau}})\Big|\bigg).

(6.4)

Our next task is to estimate the three terms on the RHS of (
6.4
) as follows.

\displaystyle\Big|\bigcup_{T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}}Y_{\rho}(T_{\rho})\Big|

\displaystyle\geq\delta^{\varepsilon_{1}}\rho^{\omega^{\prime}}\big((\#\mathbb{T}_{\rho})^{1/2}|T_{\rho}|\big)^{\sigma},

(6.5)

\displaystyle\inf_{T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}}\Big|\bigcup_{T_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}}\in\mathbb{T}^{T_{\rho}}}Y_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}}(T_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}})\Big|

\displaystyle\geq\delta^{\varepsilon_{1}}\Big(\frac{\tau}{\rho}\Big)^{\omega}\Big(\Big(\frac{\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{\rho}}\Big)^{1/2}\frac{|T_{\tau}|}{|T_{\rho}|}\Big)^{\sigma},

(6.6)

\displaystyle\inf_{T_{\tau}\in\mathbb{T}_{\tau}}\Big|\bigcup_{T^{T_{\tau}}\in\mathbb{T}^{T_{\tau}}}Y^{T_{\tau}}(T^{T_{\tau}})\Big|

\displaystyle\geq\delta^{\varepsilon_{1}}\Big(\frac{\delta}{\tau}\Big)^{\omega^{\prime}}\Big(\Big(\frac{\#\mathbb{T}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau}}\Big)^{1/2}\frac{|T|}{|T_{\tau}|}\Big)^{\sigma},

(6.7)

where
\varepsilon_{1}=\frac{\zeta_{1}}{24}(\omega-\omega^{\prime})
.

First, observe that if we choose
\eta
sufficiently small, then (
6.5
) (resp. (
6.6
) or (
6.7
)) immediately holds if
\rho>\delta^{\varepsilon_{1}/2}
(resp.
\delta/\tau>\delta^{\varepsilon_{1}}/2
). This is because the volume of the union (
6.5
) is bounded by the volume of a single tube. In particular, we have the following

•

Either (
6.5
) automatically holds, or
(\mathbb{T}_{\rho},Y_{\rho})_{\rho}
is
\rho^{O(\eta/\varepsilon_{1})}
dense and satisfies
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\rho})\lesssim\rho^{-2\zeta_{2}/\varepsilon_{1}}
.

•

Since
\varepsilon_{1}\leq\zeta_{1}/24
and
\tau\leq\delta^{\zeta_{1}/5}\rho
, each pair
(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}},Y_{\rho}^{T_{\rho}})_{\tau/\rho}
is
(\tau/\rho)^{O(\eta/\varepsilon_{1})}
dense and satisfies
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}})\lesssim(\tau/\rho)^{-2\zeta_{2}/\varepsilon_{1}}
and
\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}}\geq(\tau/\rho)^{2\zeta_{2}/\varepsilon_{1}}(\rho/\tau)^{2}
.

•

Either (
6.7
) automatically holds, or each pair
(\mathbb{T}^{T_{\tau}},Y^{T_{\tau}})_{\delta/\tau}
is
(\delta/\tau)^{O(\eta/\varepsilon_{1})}
dense and satisfies
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{T_{\tau}})\lesssim(\delta/\tau)^{-2\zeta_{2}/\varepsilon_{1}}
.

If we select
\eta
and
\zeta_{2}
sufficiently small, depending on
\omega
and
t
(recall that
t\leq\omega-\omega^{\prime}
) (
\eta
and
\zeta_{2}
also depend on
\varepsilon_{1}
, but
\varepsilon_{1}
only depends on
\zeta_{1}=\zeta_{1}(\omega)
,
\omega
and
t
), then in light of the three bullet points above, the estimates (
6.5
), (
6.6
), and (
6.7
) follow from
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega^{\prime})
,
\mathcal{D}(\sigma,\omega)
, and
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega^{\prime})
, respectively.

Combining (
6.4
), (
6.5
), (
6.6
), and (
6.7
), we conclude that

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|\geq\delta^{3\varepsilon_{1}}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\tau}\Big)^{\omega-\omega^{\prime}}\delta^{\omega^{\prime}}\Big((\#\mathbb{T})^{1/2}|T|\Big)^{\sigma}\geq\delta^{\frac{\zeta_{1}(\omega-\omega^{\prime})}{8}}\delta^{\omega^{\prime}}\Big((\#\mathbb{T})^{1/2}|T|\Big)^{\sigma}.

(6.8)

Combining (
6.3
) and (
6.8
), we verify that (
6.2
) holds, provided
\alpha(\omega,t)\leq\frac{\zeta_{1}t}{16}
;
\zeta_{4}<\frac{\zeta_{1}t}{17}
; and
\eta>0
is chosen sufficiently small. Henceforth we shall assume that Conclusion (B) of Proposition
6.3
does
not
hold.

Step 3.
Suppose that Conclusion (C) of Proposition
6.3
holds, i.e. there is a set
\mathcal{W}
of
a\times b\times 1
prisms, with
a\leq\delta^{\zeta_{2}/100}b
, so that
\mathcal{W}
satisfies the hypotheses of Proposition
5.1
(B):
\mathcal{W}
factors
\mathbb{T}
above and below with respect to the Frostman Convex Wolff Axioms with error
O(\delta^{-\zeta_{3}})
. And for each
W\in\mathcal{W}
we have
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{W}[N_{b}(W)])\lesssim\delta^{-\zeta_{3}}
.

Let
\varepsilon_{2}=\zeta_{2}\omega/200
. If
\zeta_{3}
is selected sufficiently small depending on
\zeta_{2}
and
\varepsilon_{2}
(both of these numbers in turn ultimately only depend on
\omega
and
t
) and if
\eta>0
is selected sufficiently small, then by Proposition
5.1
(B) (with
\varepsilon_{2}
in place of
\varepsilon
) we have

\begin{split}\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|&\gtrsim\delta^{\omega^{\prime}+\varepsilon_{2}}\Big(\frac{b}{a}\Big)^{\omega^{\prime}}\big((\#\mathbb{T})^{1/2}|T|\big)^{\sigma}\\
&\geq\delta^{\omega^{\prime}+\varepsilon_{2}}\delta^{-\frac{\zeta_{2}\omega}{100}}\big((\#\mathbb{T})^{1/2}|T|\big)^{\sigma}\\
&\geq\delta^{\omega^{\prime}-\frac{\zeta_{2}\omega}{200}}\big((\#\mathbb{T})^{1/2}|T|\big)^{\sigma}.\end{split}

(6.9)

Combining (
6.3
) and (
6.9
), we verify that (
6.2
) holds, provided
\alpha(\omega,t)\leq\frac{\zeta_{2}\omega}{400}
;
\zeta_{4}<\frac{\zeta_{2}\omega}{500}
; and
\eta>0
is chosen sufficiently small.

Step 4.

It remains to consider the case where every subset
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{T}
with
\#\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\geq\delta^{\eta}(\#\mathbb{T})
satisfies
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{\prime})>\delta^{-\zeta_{4}}
. Apply Proposition
4.6
(factoring a collection of convex sets) to
\mathbb{T}
, and denote the output by
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{W}
. Then Item ii) of Proposition
4.6
implies
\#\mathbb{T}[W]\approx_{\delta}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\frac{|W|}{|T|}
. Since
\#\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\geq\delta^{\eta}(\#\mathbb{T})
, we have
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{\prime})>\delta^{-\zeta_{4}}
. We also have
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{\prime W})\lessapprox_{\delta}1
for each
W\in\mathcal{W}
, from which it follows that
|W|\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\zeta_{4}}
(recall that the sets
W\in\mathcal{W}
are congruent, and thus they all have identical volume).

If the prisms in
\mathcal{W}
are flat, in the sense that they are comparable to
a\times b\times 1
prisms with
a\leq\delta^{\zeta_{5}}b
, then we can apply Proposition
5.1
(A) with
\varepsilon_{3}=\zeta_{5}\omega^{\prime}/2
to conclude that

\begin{split}\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|&\gtrsim\delta^{\omega^{\prime}+\varepsilon_{3}}\Big(\frac{b}{a}\Big)^{\omega^{\prime}}(m^{\prime})^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T}^{\prime})|T|\big((m^{\prime})^{-3/2}(\ell^{\prime})(\#\mathbb{T})|T|^{1/2}\big)^{-\sigma}\\
&\gtrapprox\delta^{\omega^{\prime}-\frac{\zeta_{5}\omega^{\prime}}{2}+2\eta}m^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|\big(m^{-3/2}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|^{1/2}\big)^{-\sigma}.\end{split}

(6.10)

In the second inequality, we used the fact that
\#\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\geq\delta^{\eta}\#\mathbb{T}
;
\ell^{\prime}:=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\lessapprox\delta^{-\eta}
;
m^{\prime}:=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\leq m
; and
\sigma\leq 2/3
. We conclude that (
6.2
) holds, provided
\alpha(\omega,t)\leq\frac{\zeta_{5}\omega}{4}\leq\frac{\zeta_{5}\omega^{\prime}}{4}
and
\eta>0
is chosen sufficiently small.

Finally, we consider the case where the prisms in
\mathcal{W}
are not flat, in the sense that
a\geq\delta^{\zeta_{5}}b
. In this case we can replace each prism
W\in\mathcal{W}
by its
b
-neighbourhood, and then refine the corresponding set of
b
-tubes
\mathbb{T}_{b}
by a factor of
(b/a)^{3}\leq\delta^{-3\zeta_{5}}
(this is the number of essentially distinct
a\times b\times 1
prisms that can fit inside a
b
tube) so that the tubes in
\mathbb{T}_{b}
are essentially distinct. Since
|W|\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\zeta_{4}}
and
a\geq\delta^{\zeta_{5}}b
, we have

b\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\zeta_{4}/2-\zeta_{5}}\leq\delta^{\zeta_{4}/3}.

(6.11)

To recap, the set
\mathbb{T}_{b}
has the following properties.

•

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{b})\lesssim\frac{b}{a}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{W})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\zeta_{5}}\leq b^{-3\zeta_{5}/\zeta_{4}}.

•

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T}_{b})\lesssim\delta^{-3\zeta_{5}}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathcal{W})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-3\zeta_{5}}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-3\zeta_{5}-\eta}
.

•

For each
T_{b}\in\mathbb{T}_{b}
, we have
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{T_{b}})\lesssim\frac{b}{a}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{W})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\zeta_{5}}
, where
\mathcal{W}\ni W\subset T_{b}
is the prism containing all of the tubes in
\mathbb{T}[T_{b}]
.

•

For each
T_{b}\in\mathbb{T}_{b}
, we have
\#\mathbb{T}^{T_{b}}\lessapprox_{\delta}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{W})\frac{b}{a}\#\mathbb{T}^{W}\lessapprox_{\delta}\frac{b}{a}m\frac{|W|}{|T|}\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-2\zeta_{5}}m\frac{|T_{b}|}{|T|}.

(For the second item above, we make crucial use of the fact that
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T})\leq\delta^{-\eta}
, which is why the conclusion of Lemma
6.4
only says that
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}(\sigma,\omega^{\prime}-\alpha)
is true, rather than the superficially stronger statement
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega^{\prime}-\alpha)
).

Mirroring the argument in Step 2, refine the shadings
Y(T)
on each set of tubes
\mathbb{T}[T_{b}]
to have average density on balls of radius
b
. We define the shading
Y_{b}(T_{b})
to be the union of those
b
-balls that intersect
\bigcup_{\mathbb{T}[T_{b}]}Y(T)
. Then
(\mathbb{T}_{b},Y_{b})_{b}
is
\delta^{\eta}\geq b^{3\eta/\zeta_{4}}
dense. We thus have the following analogue of (
6.4
):

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|\geq\bigg(\Big|\bigcup_{T_{b}\in\mathbb{T}_{b}}Y_{\rho}(T_{b})\Big|\bigg)\bigg(\inf_{T_{b}\in\mathbb{T}_{b}}\Big|\bigcup_{T^{T_{b}}\in\mathbb{T}^{T_{b}}}Y^{T_{b}}(T^{T_{b}})\Big|\bigg).

(6.12)

Let
\varepsilon_{4}=\frac{\zeta_{4}(\omega-\omega^{\prime})}{12}
. If
\zeta_{5}
and
\eta
are selected sufficiently small depending on
\zeta_{4}
and
\varepsilon_{4}
(which in turn depend on
\omega
and
t
), then we can use the estimate (
1.2
) from Assertion
\mathcal{D}(\sigma,\omega)
to conclude that

\Big|\bigcup_{T_{b}\in\mathbb{T}_{b}}Y_{\rho}(T_{b})\Big|\gtrsim b^{\omega+\varepsilon_{4}}(\#\mathbb{T}_{b})|T_{b}|\big((\#\mathbb{T}_{b})|T_{b}|^{1/2}\big)^{-\sigma}.

(6.13)

Finally, we would like to obtain the estimate

\Big|\bigcup_{T^{T_{b}}\in\mathbb{T}^{T_{b}}}Y^{T_{b}}(T^{T_{b}})\Big|\gtrsim\delta^{\varepsilon_{4}}\big(\frac{\delta}{b}\big)^{\omega^{\prime}}\Big((\#\mathbb{T}^{T_{b}})^{1/2}\frac{|T|}{|T_{b}|}\Big)^{\sigma}.

(6.14)

When
\delta/b>\delta^{\varepsilon_{4}/2}
, we have that (
6.14
) follows from the elementary fact that the shading on each
Y(T)
is
\gtrapprox\delta^{\eta}
dense, and the union on the LHS of (
6.14
) is bounded by the volume of a single tube. On the other hand, when
\delta/b\leq\delta^{\varepsilon_{4}/2}
, we have that (
6.14
) follows from the estimate (
1.3
) from Assertion
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega^{\prime})
, provided
\zeta_{5}
is chosen sufficiently small depending on
\zeta_{4}
and
\varepsilon_{4}
, since
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{T_{b}})\lessapprox\delta^{-\zeta_{5}}
.

Since
\#\mathbb{T}^{T_{b}}\lessapprox m\delta^{-2\zeta_{5}}\frac{|T|}{|T_{b}|}
for each
T_{b}\in\mathbb{T}_{b}
(where
m=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})
), (
6.14
) becomes

\Big|\bigcup_{T^{T_{b}}\in\mathbb{T}^{T_{b}}}Y^{T_{b}}(T^{T_{b}})\Big|\gtrsim\delta^{\varepsilon_{4}+2\zeta_{5}}\big(\frac{\delta}{b}\big)^{\omega^{\prime}}m^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T}^{T_{b}})\frac{|T|}{|T_{b}|}\Big(m^{-3/2}(\#\mathbb{T}^{T_{b}})\Big(\frac{|T|}{|T_{b}|}\Big)^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma}.

(6.15)

Combining (
6.12
), (
6.13
), (
6.15
), and (
6.11
), we conclude that if we select
\zeta_{5}\leq\zeta_{4}(\omega-\omega^{\prime})/12
, then

\begin{split}\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|&\geq\delta^{\omega^{\prime}+\varepsilon_{4}+2\zeta_{5}}b^{\omega-\omega^{\prime}+\varepsilon_{4}}m^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|\Big(m^{-3/2}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma}\\
&\geq\delta^{\omega^{\prime}-\frac{\zeta_{4}(\omega-\omega^{\prime})}{12}}m^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|\Big(m^{-3/2}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma}.\end{split}

(6.16)

We conclude that (
6.2
) holds, provided
\alpha(\omega,t)\leq\frac{\zeta_{4}t}{12},
and
\eta>0
is chosen sufficiently small.
∎

We now use Lemma
6.4
to prove Proposition
1.6
.

Proof of Proposition
1.6
.

Let
0\leq\sigma\leq 2/3,\ \omega\geq 0,
and suppose that Assertion
\mathcal{D}(\sigma,\omega)
is true. Fix
t>0
and let
\alpha=\alpha(\sigma,\omega,t)>0
be the output of
Lemma
6.4
. Since a
\delta
-tube has volume
\sim\delta^{2}
, we always have that
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,2)
is true. Now suppose that
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega^{\prime})
is true for some
\omega^{\prime}\in[\omega+t,2]
. Applying Lemma
6.4
followed by Proposition
5.14
, we have

\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega^{\prime})\implies\tilde{\mathcal{E}}(\sigma,\omega^{\prime}-\alpha)\implies\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega^{\prime}-\alpha).

Iterating the above argument, we conclude that
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega^{\prime\prime})
is true for some
\omega^{\prime\prime}\leq\omega+t
, so in particular
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega+t)
is true.

However,
t>0
was arbitrary, and by the definition of
\mathcal{E}
, it is clear that the set

\{\omega^{\prime}\in[\omega,2]\colon\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega^{\prime})\ \textrm{is true}\}

is a closed interval. We conclude that
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega)
is true.
∎

This concludes the proof of Proposition
1.6
, except that we must still prove Proposition
6.3
. We do this below.

6.1
Proof of Proposition
6.3
: A factoring trichotomy

Step 1.

We begin by regularizing the set
\mathbb{T}
. Let
\eta>0
be a small quantity to be determined below, with
N=1/\eta
an integer. Define
\delta_{i}=\delta^{i/N},\ i=1,\ldots,N
. By iterated pigeonholing and replacing
\mathbb{T}
by a
|\log\delta|^{-N}
-refinement, we may suppose that for each
i=1,\ldots,N
there exists a set
\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
of
\delta_{i}
-tubes that is a balanced partitioning cover of
\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i+1}}
. We will call numbers of the form
\delta_{i}
“admissible scales.” In particular, for each admissible scale
\delta_{i}
, we have

\#\mathbb{T}[T_{\delta_{i}}]\approx_{\delta}\frac{\#\mathbb{T}\phantom{{}_{\delta_{i}}}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}}\quad\textrm{for every}\ T_{\delta_{i}}\in\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}.

(6.17)

Next we apply Proposition
4.6
to each set
\mathbb{T}^{T_{\delta_{i}}}
; the output of Proposition
4.6
is a
\approx 1
refinement of
\mathbb{T}^{T_{\delta_{i}}}
(this induces a
\approx 1
refinement of
\mathbb{T}
and all sets
\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{j}}
for
j>i
; observe that (
6.17
) remains true after this refinement; to simplify notation, we still use
\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
and
\mathbb{T}
to denote these refinement) and a set
\mathcal{W}
of convex subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. If
|W|\geq\delta^{\zeta_{1}/2}
, then
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{T_{\delta_{i}}})\leq\delta^{-\zeta_{1}/2}
. In this case, we say
\mathbb{T}^{T_{\delta_{i}}}
is of
Type 1
. If
|W|<\delta^{\zeta_{1}/2}
, then we say we say
\mathbb{T}^{T_{\delta_{i}}}
is of
Type 2
.

We now proceed as follows. For each
i=N-1,\ldots,1
, if at least half the sets
\mathbb{T}^{T_{\delta_{i}}},\ T_{\delta_{i}}\in\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
are of Type 1, then we refine
\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
to only consist of those
T_{\delta_{i}}
for which
\mathbb{T}^{T_{\delta_{i}}}
are of Type 1; observe that (
6.17
) remains true after this refinement. We say that
\mathbb{T}
has passed stage
i
. On the other hand, if at least half the sets
\mathbb{T}^{T_{\delta_{i}}},\ T_{\delta_{i}}\in\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
are of Type 2, then we say that
\mathbb{T}
has failed stage
i
.

Suppose that
\mathbb{T}
passes every stage
i=N-1,\ldots,1
. Then by (
6.17
) we have that for each
i=1,\ldots,N
, the set
\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
(this consists of those
\delta_{i}
-tubes that survived the refinements described above) is a
\approx_{\delta}1
-balanced partitioning cover of
\mathbb{T}
. Furthermore, since
\mathbb{T}
passed stage
i
, we have that for each
T_{\delta_{i}}\in\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
we have that
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{T_{\delta_{i}}})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\zeta_{1}/2}.
We conclude that
\mathbb{T}
satisfies the Frostman Convex Wolff Axioms at every scale with error
O(\delta^{-\zeta_{1}})
, and hence Conclusion (A) of Proposition
6.3
holds.

Step 2.

Suppose that
\mathbb{T}
fails some stage
i\geq 1
. After pigeonholing and replacing
\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
and
\mathbb{T}
by a
\approx_{\delta}1
refinement, we may suppose that there exists
\delta\leq a\leq b\leq 1
so that for each
T_{\delta_{i}}\in\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
, the output of Proposition
4.6
applied to
\mathbb{T}^{T_{\delta_{i}}}
consists of a set
\mathcal{W}_{T_{\delta_{i}}}
of
\frac{a}{\delta_{i}}\times\frac{b}{\delta_{i}}\times 1
prisms that forms a
\approx_{\delta}1
balanced cover of
\mathbb{T}^{T_{\delta_{i}}}
, and factors
\mathbb{T}^{T_{\delta_{i}}}
from above (resp. below) with respect to the Katz-Tao Convex Wolff Axioms (resp. Frostman Convex Wolff Axioms) with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}1
.

Since the tubes in
\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
are essentially distinct, we can further refine
\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
by a
\sim 1
factor so that every pair of distinct tubes
T_{\delta_{i}},T_{\delta_{i}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
that intersect must satisfy
\angle\big(\mathrm{dir}(T_{\delta_{i}}),\ \mathrm{dir}(T_{\delta_{i}}^{\prime})\big)\geq 100\delta_{i}
, so in particular we have

\operatorname{diam}(2T_{\delta_{i}}\cap 2T_{\delta_{i}}^{\prime})\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(6.18)

Define

\mathcal{W}=\bigsqcup_{T_{\delta_{i}}\in\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}}\phi_{T_{\delta_{i}}}^{-1}\big(\mathcal{W}_{T_{\delta_{i}}}\big).

Then
\mathcal{W}
is a collection of convex sets, each of which is comparable to a
a\times b\times 1
prism. Recall that since
\mathbb{T}
failed stage
i
, we have that the prisms in
\mathcal{W}
are substantially smaller than the tubes in
\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
; specifically, we have

|W|\lesssim\delta^{\zeta_{1}/2}|T_{\delta_{i}}|.

(6.19)

We claim that the convex sets in
\mathcal{W}
are essentially distinct. To verify this claim, we argue as follows. Every pair of convex sets
W,W^{\prime}
from the same set
\mathcal{W}_{T_{\delta_{i}}}
are essentially distinct. On the other hand, if
W\in\mathcal{W}_{T_{\delta_{i}}}
and
W^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}_{T_{\delta_{i}}^{\prime}}
for distinct tubes
T_{\delta_{i}}
and
T_{\delta_{i}}^{\prime}
, then
\mathrm{diam}(W\cap W^{\prime})\leq\mathrm{diam}(T_{\delta_{i}}\cap T_{\delta_{i}}^{\prime})\leq\frac{1}{2}
by (
6.18
), from which it follows that
W
and
W^{\prime}
are essentially distinct.

Since
\mathcal{W}
is a
\approx_{\delta}1
balanced cover of
\mathbb{T}
, and
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\eta}
, by Remark
4.3
(A) (Frostman Wolff Axioms are inherited upwards), we have
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{W})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\eta}
; we will select
\eta>0
sufficiently small so that
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{W})\lesssim\delta^{-\zeta_{3}}
.

Step 3.
Suppose that the prisms in
\mathcal{W}
are flat, in the sense that
a\leq\delta^{\zeta_{2}/100}b
. Our task is to show that
\mathcal{W}
and our refined set
\mathbb{T}
satisfies the conditions of Conclusion (C) from Proposition
6.3
.

Each
W\in\mathcal{W}
came from some set
\mathcal{W}_{T_{\delta_{i}}}
. We claim that if
W^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}
satisfies
W^{\prime}\subset N_{b}(W)
, then we must have that
W^{\prime}
came from the same set
\mathcal{W}_{T_{\delta_{i}}}
, i.e.

\mathcal{W}[N_{b}(W)]=(\mathcal{W}[T_{\delta_{i}}])[N_{b}(W)]=\phi_{T_{\delta_{i}}}^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{T_{\delta_{i}}})[N_{b}(W)].

(6.20)

To verify this claim, we argue by contradiction: suppose instead that
W^{\prime}
came from a distinct tube
T_{\delta_{i}}^{\prime}
, then we would have
W^{\prime}\subset N_{b}(W)\cap T_{\delta_{i}}^{\prime}\subset 2T_{\delta_{i}}\cap T_{\delta_{i}}^{\prime},
and in particular the latter set would have diameter
\geq 1
. But this is forbidden by (
6.18
).

(
6.20
) implies that

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}\big(\mathcal{W}[N_{b}(W)]\big)=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}\big((\mathcal{W}[T_{\delta_{i}}])[N_{b}(W)]\big)\leq C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{W}_{T_{\rho_{i}}})\lessapprox_{\delta}1.

Thus
\mathcal{W}
and our refined set
\mathbb{T}
satisfies the conditions of Conclusion (C) from Proposition
6.3
.

Step 4.

Now we consider the case where the prisms in
\mathcal{W}
are not flat, in the sense that
a>\delta^{\zeta_{2}/100}b
. Define
\tau
to be the smallest admissible scale greater than or equal to
b
. Since at most
O((b/a)^{3})
essentially distinct
a\times b\times 1
prisms can fit inside a
b
-tube, and at most
O(\delta^{-4/N})=O(\delta^{-4\eta})
essentially distinct
b
-tubes can fit inside a
\tau
-tube, we have that
\mathcal{W}[T_{\tau}]\lesssim\delta^{-4\eta}(b/a)^{3}\lesssim\delta^{-4\zeta_{2}/100}
for every
\tau
-tube
T_{\tau}
(for the last inequality we select
\eta\leq\zeta_{2}/400
). After pigeonholing
\mathbb{T},\mathcal{W}
, and
\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
, we may suppose that
\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
is a balanced partitioning cover of
\mathcal{W}
. We have

1\leq\#\mathcal{W}[T_{\tau}]\lesssim\delta^{-4\zeta_{2}/100}\quad\textrm{for each}\ T_{\tau}\in\mathbb{T}_{\tau}.

(6.21)

Since
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}[W])\lessapprox_{\delta}1
for each
W\in\mathcal{W}
, we conclude from (
6.21
) that
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}[T_{\tau}])\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-4\zeta_{2}/100}
for each
T_{\tau}\in\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
; this gives us Conclusion (B.i).

At this point, we have correctly identified the scale
\tau
from Conclusion (B) of Proposition
6.3
. What about the scale
\rho
? One candidate is
\delta_{i}
; by (
6.19
) we have
\tau\lesssim\delta^{\zeta_{1}/4-2\zeta_{2}/100}\delta_{i}\leq\delta^{\zeta_{1}/5}\delta_{i}
, as specified in Conclusion (B).

The scale
\delta_{i}
satisfies some of the required properties of Conclusion (B). Recall that for each
T_{\delta_{i}}\in\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
, we have
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{W}[T_{\delta_{i}}])\lessapprox_{\delta}1
. Since
|T_{\tau}|=(b/a)|W|\leq\delta^{-\zeta_{2}/100}|W|
, by (
6.21
) we have

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}\big(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}[T_{\delta_{i}}]\big)\leq\delta^{-5\zeta_{2}/100}\quad\textrm{for each}\ T_{\delta_{i}}\in\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}.

(6.22)

This is half of Conclusion (B.ii). If
\frac{\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}}\geq\delta^{100\eta}(\delta_{i}/\tau)^{2}
(in fact a weaker estimate
\frac{\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}}\geq\delta^{\zeta_{2}}(\delta_{i}/\tau)^{2}
suffices), then after a refinement,
\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
satisfies Conclusion (B.ii). Conclusion (B.iii) then follows from Remark
4.3
(A) (Frostman Wolff Axioms are inherited upwards), and we are done.

Suppose instead that
\frac{\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}}<\delta^{100\eta}(\delta_{i}/\tau)^{2}
. Let
\rho
be the minimum of all admissible scales in
[\delta_{i},1]
for which

\frac{\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{\rho}}\geq\delta^{100\eta}(\rho/\tau)^{2}.

(6.23)

Such a choice of
\rho\in[\rho_{i},1]
must exist, since
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\eta},
and hence
\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau}\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\eta}\tau^{-2},
from which it follows that
\rho=1
satisfies (
6.23
) and hence
\rho=1
is a valid candidate.

In particular, for this choice of
\rho
we have

\delta^{100\eta}(\rho/\tau)^{2}\leq\frac{\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{\rho}}\leq\delta^{50\eta}(\rho/\tau)^{2},

(6.24)

since if the RHS of (
6.24
) failed, then (
6.23
) would hold for a smaller admissible scale, which would contradict the minimality of
\rho
.

Suppose for the moment that there exists a
\gtrapprox_{\delta}1
-refinement of
\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
(abusing notation, we will continue to call this set
\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
) such that

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}\big(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}}\big)\leq\delta^{-\zeta_{2}/2}\quad\textrm{for at least half of the tubes}\ T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}.

(6.25)

Then (
6.24
) plus (
6.25
) implies that Conclusion (B.ii) holds, and Conclusion (B.iii) follows from Remark
4.3
(A) (Frostman Wolff axioms are inherited upwards). Thus if (
6.25
) is true, then Conclusion (B) of Proposition
6.3
holds, and we are done.

Step 5.
We claim that either (
6.25
) holds (and we are done, as discussed in the previous step), or else Conclusion (C) of Proposition
6.3
holds.

We will verify this claim as follows. Suppose that (
6.25
) fails.
Applying Proposition
4.6
to each set
\mathbb{T}^{T_{\rho}}
and then undoing the scaling
\phi_{T_{\rho}}
, we obtain a refinement of
\mathbb{T}[T_{\rho}]
(abusing notation, we will continue to call this set
\mathbb{T}[T_{\rho}]
), and a set
\mathcal{U}_{T_{\rho}}
of
s\times t\times 1
prisms contained in
T_{\rho}
(so in particular
t\leq\rho
) that factors
\mathbb{T}[T_{\rho}]
from below with respect to the Frostman Convex Wolff axioms with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}1
and each
T\in\mathbb{T}[T_{\rho}]
is contained in
\lessapprox_{\delta}1
sets
U\in\mathcal{U}_{T_{\rho}}
.

Pigeonhole and refine
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
to consist of those tubes
T_{\rho}
for which
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}\big(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}}\big)>\delta^{-\zeta_{2}/2}
(such a refinement exists because (
6.25
) fails) and the corresponding sets
\mathcal{U}_{T_{\rho}}
are comparable to
s\times t\times 1
prisms for a common pair of numbers
(s,t)
. In particular, this implies

|U|\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\zeta_{2}/2}|T_{\rho}|.

(6.26)

(C.f. (
6.19
) when
s\geq\tau
. When
s\leq\tau
, this is true because
\tau\lesssim\delta^{\zeta_{1}/5}\delta_{i}\leq\delta^{\zeta_{1}/5}\rho
).

Suppose for the moment that
s\geq\tau
. Then
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{U})\lessapprox_{\delta}1
by Remark
4.3
(A) (Frostman Wolff axioms are inherited upwards). By (
6.24
) and our hypothesis that
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{T_{\rho}})>\delta^{-\zeta_{2}/2}
, we have

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}[T_{\rho}])\geq\delta^{-\zeta_{2}/2+100\eta}.

(6.27)

We conclude that by Item ii) of Proposition
4.6
,

\#(\mathbb{T}[T_{\rho}])[U]\gtrapprox_{\delta}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}[T_{\rho}])\Big(\frac{|U|}{|T|}\Big)\quad\textrm{for each}\ U\in\mathcal{U}_{T_{\rho}}.

Since
s\geq\tau
and
\tau
is an admissible scale, we have

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}[T_{\rho}])\gtrapprox_{\delta}\frac{\#\mathbb{T}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau}}\frac{|T|}{|T_{\tau}|}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}[T_{\rho}]),

and so

\#(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}[T_{\rho}])[U]\gtrapprox_{\delta}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}\big(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}[T_{\rho}]\big)\Big(\frac{|U|}{|T_{\tau}|}\Big)\geq\delta^{-\zeta_{2}/2+100\eta}\Big(\frac{|U|}{|T_{\tau}|}\Big)\quad\textrm{for each}\ U\in\mathcal{U}_{T_{\rho}}.

(6.28)

We will show that

s<\delta^{\zeta_{2}/100}t.

(6.29)

To verify (
6.29
), suppose to the contrary that
s\geq\delta^{\zeta_{2}/100}t
; we will obtain a contradiction. If
s\geq\delta^{\zeta_{2}/100}t
then
t\leq\delta^{\zeta_{2}/4}\rho
. By (
6.28
) and the fact that each tube in
\mathbb{T}_{\tau}[T_{\rho}]
is contained in
\lessapprox_{\delta}1
sets
U\in\mathcal{U}_{T_{\rho}}
, we have

\#\mathcal{U}_{T_{\rho}}\lessapprox_{\delta}\frac{\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau}[T_{\rho}]}{\inf_{U\in\mathcal{U}_{T_{\rho}}}\#(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}[T_{\rho}])[U]}\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\zeta_{2}/2-100\eta}(\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau}[T_{\rho}])\frac{|T_{\tau}|}{|U|}\leq\delta^{\frac{48}{100}\zeta_{2}}(\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau}[T_{\rho}])\big(\frac{\tau}{t}\big)^{2},

and thus if
t^{\prime}
is the smallest admissible scale greater than or equal to
t
, then

\#\mathbb{T}_{t^{\prime}}\leq\#\Big(\bigcup_{T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}}\mathcal{U}_{T_{\rho}}\Big)\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\frac{48}{100}\zeta_{2}}(\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau})\big(\frac{\tau}{t}\big)^{2}\leq\delta^{\frac{47}{100}\zeta_{2}}(\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau})\big(\frac{\tau}{t^{\prime}}\big)^{2}.

(6.30)

From (
6.30
) we see that
t^{\prime}\geq\delta_{i}
— if not, then there would exist a
t^{\prime}
-tube
T_{t^{\prime}}
with
\#\mathbb{T}_{\tau}[T_{t^{\prime}}]\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\frac{47}{100}\zeta_{2}}\frac{|T_{t^{\prime}}|}{|T_{\tau}|}
, and this
t^{\prime}
-tube would be contained in some
\delta_{i}
-tube; but this violates (
6.22
).

Comparing (
6.30
) and (
6.23
), we see that
t^{\prime}\in[t,\delta^{\zeta_{2}/5}\rho]\subset[\delta_{i},\delta^{\zeta_{2}/5}\rho]
is an admissible scale that satisfies (
6.24
). But this contradicts the assumption that
\rho
was the minimal such scale. We conclude that (
6.29
) must hold.

When (
6.29
) holds, we are in precisely the same situation as the beginning of Step 3:
The set of flat (recall (
6.29
)) prisms
\mathcal{U}=\bigcup_{T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}}\mathcal{U}_{T_{\rho}}
plays the role of
\mathcal{W}
, and the set of essentially distinct
\rho
-tubes
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
plays the role of
\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
. An identical argument with the same numerology (up to harmless
\delta^{\eta}
factors) shows that Conclusion (C) of Proposition
6.3
holds.

Now suppose (
6.29
) does not hold, so
\delta^{\zeta_{2}/100}t\leq s<\tau
. If
s\geq\tau\delta^{\zeta_{2}/20}
, then together with
s\geq\delta^{\zeta_{2}/100}t
,

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}[T_{\rho}])\leq\delta^{-\zeta_{2}/4}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{U}_{T_{\rho}})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\zeta_{2}/4},

which is a contradiction to (
6.27
). So we may assume
s\leq\tau\delta^{\zeta_{2}/20}
and
s\geq\delta^{\zeta_{2}/100}t
, and we are in the same situation as the beginning of Step 4 with
(s,t)
in place of
(a,b)
,
\mathcal{U}
in place of
\mathcal{W}
, and
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
in place of
\mathbb{T}_{\delta_{i}}
. However, we have the additional condition that
s/\rho\leq\delta^{\zeta_{2}/20}\tau/\delta_{i}
; this means that the prisms in
\mathcal{U}
are substantially flatter than the prisms in
\mathcal{W}
. We return to the beginning of Step 4 and repeat the argument; we iterate this process until either Conclusion (B) or Conclusion (C) holds; this must occur after at most
20/\zeta_{2}
iterations. Note that each iteration of this process induces a
\approx_{\delta}1
refinement of
\mathbb{T}
, etc. but since this process repeats at most
20/\zeta_{2}
times, this refinement is harmless.

7
A two-scale grains decomposition for tubes in
\mathbb{R}^{3}

In
[
16
]
, Katz, Łaba and Tao proved that every union of
\delta
tubes in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
coming from the discretization of a (hypothetical) Kakeya set with upper Minkowski dimension
5/2
can be written as a union of “grains,” (i.e. rectangular prisms) of dimensions roughly
\delta\times\delta^{1/2}\times\delta^{1/2}
. Guth
[
9
]
generalized this result and proved that every union of
\delta
tubes in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfying a certain broadness hypothesis can be written as a union of grains of dimensions roughly
\delta\times t\times t
, where the diameter
t
is related to the number of tubes in the arrangement and the volume of their union.

The purpose of this section is to prove a structural statement for unions of
\delta
tubes in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, in the spirit of the Katz-Łaba-Tao and Guth results described above. This is Proposition
7.5
below. As discussed in the introduction, Proposition
7.5
is a key step in the proof of Proposition
1.7
—Proposition
7.5
helps us find the correct scales and arrangements of convex sets to which we can apply Assertion
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega)
.

In brief, Proposition
7.5
explores what happens when we cover an arrangement of
\delta
-tubes by
\rho
-tubes, apply (a variant of) Guth’s grains theorem inside each re-scaled
\rho
-tube, and then analyze how the resulting grains coming from the
\delta
tubes inside different
\rho
-tubes interact. The specific hypotheses and conclusions of Proposition
7.5
are somewhat technical; they were adapted to match the needs of the arguments in Section
9
. In order to state Proposition
7.5
we will require a few definitions.

Definition 7.1
.

Let
\lambda>0
,
0<\delta\leq\rho\leq 1
and
\delta\leq a\leq b\leq c
with
\rho=b/c
. Let
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
be a set of
\delta
tubes and their associated shading, and let
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
be a balanced partitioning cover of
\mathbb{T}
. We say
(\mathcal{P},Y)_{a\times b\times c}
is a
robustly
\lambda
-dense two-scale grains decomposition of
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
with regard to (wrt)
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
if the following is true:

(i)

For each
P\in\mathcal{P}
, there is a unique
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
satisfying
P\subset T_{\rho}
and
\angle(\mathrm{dir}(P),\mathrm{dir}(T_{\rho}))\leq 2\rho
. This induces a partition
\mathcal{P}=\bigsqcup_{\mathbb{T}_{\rho}}\mathcal{P}_{T_{\rho}}
.

(ii)

For each
T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
, the sets
\{Y(P)\colon P\in P\in\mathcal{P}_{T_{\rho}}\}
are disjoint, and we have

\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}[T_{\rho}]}Y(T)=\bigsqcup_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{T_{\rho}}}Y(P).

(7.1)

(iii)

The pair
(\mathcal{P},Y)_{a\times b\times c}
is
\lambda
-dense, and furthermore there exists a number
\mu
so that for each
T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
and each
x\in\eqref{equalityOfTubesGrains}
, we have
\#\mathbb{T}[T_{\rho}]_{Y}(x)\sim\mu
.

(iv)

For each
T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
and each pair
T\in\mathbb{T}[T_{\rho}]
and
P\in\mathcal{P}_{T_{\rho}}
with
Y(T)\cap Y(P)\neq\emptyset
, we have that
T
exits
P
through the “long end” (See Figure
9
), and
Y(T)\cap P\subset Y(P)
.

Figure 9:
A tube exiting a grain through the long ends (left) vs failing to do so (middle and right).

Remark 7.2
.

Conclusion (iv) implies that
a\geq 2\delta
. Usually we will be interested in the case where
a\sim\delta
, though it will sometimes be useful to consider larger values of
a
.

Definition 7.3
.

Let
P
be a
a\times b\times c
prism. Define
\square(P)
to be the
\frac{ac}{b}\times c\times c
prism containing
P
with the same center and normal direction as
P
(the latter condition means that
\Pi(P)=\Pi(\square(P))
. Observe that both
\Pi(P)
and
\Pi(\square(P))
are defined up to accuracy
a/b
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a set of
a\times b\times c
prisms, and let
W
be a convex set. Define

\mathcal{P}\langle W\rangle=\{P\in\mathcal{P}\colon\square(P)\subset W\}.

Note that since
P\subset\square(P)
, we have
\mathcal{P}\langle W\rangle\subset\mathcal{P}[W]
.

Observe that if
P,P^{\prime}
are intersecting
a\times b\times c
prisms and
\angle(\Pi(P),\Pi(P^{\prime}))\leq K\frac{a}{b}
for some
K\geq 1
, then the
K
-fold thickenings (i.e. the prisms of dimensions
\frac{ac}{b}\times c\times c
with the same center, direction, and tangent plane) of
\square(P)
and
\square(P^{\prime})
are comparable up to factors of
K
. In particular, if
(\mathcal{P},Y)_{a\times b\times c}
is a pair of prisms and their associated shadings, with
\angle(\Pi(P),\Pi(P^{\prime}))\leq K\frac{a}{b}
for all pairs
P,P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}
for which
Y(P)\cap Y(P^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
, then we can find a set
\mathcal{W}
of prisms of dimensions comparable to
K\frac{ac}{b}\times c\times c
so that each
P\in\mathcal{P}
is contained in at least 1, and at most
O(1)
sets of the form
\mathcal{P}\langle W\rangle,\ W\in\mathcal{W}.
Furthermore, the sets
\big\{\bigcup_{P\in\mathcal{P}\langle W\rangle}Y(P),\ W\in\mathcal{W}\big\}
are
O(1)
overlapping.

In our arguments below, we will exploit the above observation when
K\leq\delta^{-\varepsilon}
for a small
\varepsilon>0
. Since we will analyze each set
\mathcal{P}\langle W\rangle
individually, we will be interested in the quantity
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{P}\langle W\rangle)
, rather than the (potentially much larger) quantity
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{P})
. Each set
W
contains at most
100K^{100}
essentially distinct prisms of the form
\square(P)
, and thus if
K
is not too large then
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{P}\langle W\rangle)
is controlled by
\sup_{P}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{P}\langle 2\square(P)\rangle)
, where
2\square(P)
denotes the 2-fold dilate of
\square(P)
. This motivates the following definition.

Definition 7.4
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a set of
a\times b\times c
prisms. Define

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathcal{P})=\max_{P\in\mathcal{P}}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}\big(\mathcal{P}\langle 2\square(P)\rangle\big).

With these definitions, we can now state the main result of Section
7
.

Proposition 7.5
.

Let
\omega>0,\ \sigma\in(0,2/3]
, and
\zeta\in(0,\omega/1000)
. Suppose that
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega)
is true. Then there exists
\alpha,\eta,\kappa>0
so that the following holds for all
\delta>0
. Let
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
be
\delta^{\eta}
dense, with
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})\leq\delta^{-\eta}
and
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T})\leq\delta^{-\eta}
. Then at least one of the following must hold.

(A)

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|\geq\kappa\delta^{\omega-\alpha}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|\big((\#\mathbb{T})|T|^{1/2}\big)^{-\sigma}.

(B)

There exist the following:

–

Numbers
\rho
and
\delta\leq a\leq b\leq c\leq 1
, with
\rho=b/c
.

–

A
\delta^{\zeta}
refinement
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
of
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
.

–

A set
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
of
\rho
tubes.

–

A pair
(\mathcal{G},Y)_{a\times b\times c}
.

These objects have the following properties:

(i)

(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
is
\delta^{\zeta}
dense,
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\leq\delta^{-\zeta}
, and
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\leq\delta^{-\zeta}
.

(ii)

\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
is a balanced partitioning cover of
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
that factors
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
above and below with respect to the Frostman Slab Wolff Axioms with error
\delta^{-\zeta}
.

(iii)

(\mathcal{G},Y)_{a\times b\times c}
is a robustly
\delta^{\zeta}
-dense two-scale grains decomposition of
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
wrt
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
.

(iv)

c\geq\delta^{\zeta}\frac{\rho}{\delta}\frac{\#\mathbb{T}_{\rho}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{\delta}}.

(7.2)

(v)

\delta^{1-\omega/100}\leq\rho\leq\delta^{\omega/100}
.

(vi)

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathcal{G})\leq\delta^{-\zeta}
.

An overview for the proof of Proposition
7.5
is outlined in Section
2.2
.

Fixing
\omega
and
\sigma
. In our proof of Proposition
7.5
, the values of
\omega>0
,
\sigma\in(0,2/3]
and
\zeta>0
will never change. Thus to simplify our exposition below, we will fix values of
\bm{\omega}
,
\bm{\sigma},
and
\bm{\zeta}
, which will remain unchanged throughout Sections
7
and
8
. In particular, some of our definitions (such as Definition
7.6
, which defines broadness) will depend on these quantities. By fixing them in advance, we can suppress this dependence.

As previewed in Section
2.2
, we will make crucial use Guth’s methods to find a grains decomposition inside each re-scaled set
\mathbb{T}^{T_{\rho}}
. In order to apply Guth’s techniques, the tubes need to satisfy a “broadness” condition. We describe this below.

7.1
Broadness

For the definition that follows, we will fix
\bm{\beta}=\bm{\omega}\bm{\zeta}/100.

Definition 7.6
.

Let
\delta>0
and
K\geq 1
. We say a multi-set
\mathcal{V}\subset S^{n-1}
of unit vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
is
broad with error
K
at scales
\geq\delta
if for all unit vectors
v_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and all
r\in[\delta,1]
we have

\#\{v\in\mathcal{V}\colon\angle(v,v_{0})\leq r\}\leq Kr^{\bm{\beta}}(\#\mathcal{V}).

More generally, let
\mathcal{V}
be contained in a disk
D\subset S^{n-1}
of radius
\rho
. We say
\mathcal{V}
is broad with error
K
at scales
\geq\delta
inside
D
if for all unit vectors
v_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and all
r\in[\delta,\rho]
we have

\#\{v\in\mathcal{V}\colon\angle(v,v_{0})\leq r\}\leq K(r/\rho)^{\bm{\beta}}(\#\mathcal{V}).

Definition 7.7
.

Let
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
be a collection of tubes and their associated shading, and let
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
be a cover of
\mathbb{T}
.

(A)

We say that
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
is
broad with error
K
if for each
x\in\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)
, the set of unit vectors
\{\mathrm{dir}(T)\colon x\in Y(T)\}
is broad with error
K
at scales
\geq\delta
.

(B)

We say that
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
is
broad with error
K
relative to the cover
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
if for each
T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
, the set
(\mathbb{T}^{T_{\rho}},Y^{T_{\rho}})_{\delta/\rho}
is broad with error
K
.

The next result is a variant of the “two ends” reduction. In general, a set
\mathcal{V}
of unit vectors need not be broad (with small error). However, the next result says that every set of unit vectors is broad when localized inside
\rho
disks, for some value of
\rho
. The precise statement is as follows.

Lemma 7.8
.

Let
\delta>0
and let
\mathcal{V}
be a set of vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
pointing in
\delta
-separated directions. Then there exists a scale
\rho\in[\delta,1]
; a set
\mathcal{B}
of disjoint balls
B\subset S^{n-1}
of radius
\rho
; and sets
\mathcal{V}_{B}\subset\mathcal{V}\cap B
so that the following holds.

(i)

Each set
\mathcal{V}_{B}
has cardinality
\gtrsim\rho^{\bm{\beta}}(\#\mathcal{V})
.

(ii)

\mathcal{V}_{B}
is broad with error
100
at scales
\geq\delta
inside
B
.

(iii)

\bigcup_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{V}_{B}\gtrsim(\log 1/\delta)^{-1}(\#\mathcal{V})
.

Proof.

We will greedily construct a sequence of sets
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{V}_{0}\supset\mathcal{V}_{1}\supset\ldots
as follows. For each index
i\geq 1
, let
v_{i}
be a unit vector and
r_{i}\in[\delta,1]
a radius that maximizes the quantity

r_{i}^{-\bm{\beta}}(\#\mathcal{V}_{i-1}\cap B(v_{i},r_{i})).

(7.3)

Let
\mathcal{W}_{i}=\mathcal{V}_{i-1}\cap B(v_{i},r_{i})
and let
\mathcal{V}_{i}=\mathcal{V}_{i-1}\backslash B(v_{i},\ 100r_{i})
. Note that
\#(\mathcal{V}_{i-1}\cap B(v_{i},3r_{i}))\leq 100^{\bm{\beta}}(\#\mathcal{W}_{i})
. Continue this process until
\#\mathcal{V}_{i}\leq\frac{1}{2}\#\mathcal{V}
.

We claim that for each index
i
, each unit vector
v
, and each
r\geq\delta
, we have

\#(\mathcal{W}_{i}\cap B(v,r))\leq 100(r/r_{i})^{\bm{\beta}}(\#\mathcal{W}_{i}).

(7.4)

This will give Conclusion (ii). To verify (
7.4
), suppose to the contrary that (
7.4
) failed for some pair
(v,r)
, then this would contradict the maximality of
(v_{i},r_{i})
in (
7.3
).

Note as well that since
r_{i}=1
is a valid choice of
r
, we have
\#\mathcal{W}_{i}\geq r_{i}^{\bm{\beta}}(\#\mathcal{V}_{i-1})\geq\frac{1}{2}r_{i}^{\bm{\beta}}(\#\mathcal{V})
. Finally, note that if
r_{i}\leq r_{j}\leq 2r_{i}
, then the balls
2B(v_{i},r_{i})
and
2B(v_{j},r_{j})
must be disjoint. Indeed, we may suppose that both
\mathcal{W}_{i}
and
\mathcal{W}_{j}
are non-empty. If
i<j
then we must have
B(v_{j},r_{j})\cap B(v_{i},100r_{i})^{c}\neq\emptyset
, while if
j<i
then we must have
B(v_{i},r_{i})\cap B(v_{i},100r_{j})^{c}\neq\emptyset
. In either case, since
r_{i}\leq r_{j}\leq 2r_{i}
, this means that
2B(v_{i},r_{i})\cap 2B(v_{j},r_{j})=\emptyset
.

To conclude the proof, use dyadic pigeonholing to select a scale
\rho\in[\delta,1]
so that
\#\bigcup_{i\colon\rho/2\leq r_{i}\leq\rho}\mathcal{W}_{i}\gtrsim(\log 1/\delta)^{-1}(\#\mathcal{V})
. This gives us the collection
\mathcal{B}
of disjoint balls (it is harmless for us to replace each ball
B(v_{i},r_{i})
with
B(v_{i},\rho)
; as noted above, the balls remain disjoint).
∎

Remark 7.9
.

Lemma
7.8
is similar to the standard “two-ends” broadness reduction. However, the standard two-ends broadness reduction typically replaces
\mathcal{V}
by
\mathcal{V}\cap B
for a
single

\rho
ball
B
. The resulting set has cardinality
\gtrsim\rho^{\bm{\beta}}(\#\mathcal{V})
. For our applications, this would have introduced an unacceptably large reduction in the cardinality of
\mathcal{V}
.

Corollary 7.10
.

Let
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
be a set of
\delta
tubes and their corresponding shading. Then there exists a
\gtrapprox_{\delta}1
refinement
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
of
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
; a scale
\rho\in[\delta,1]
; and a balanced partitioning cover
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
of
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})
, so that the following holds:

(i)

Each point
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
is contained
\lessapprox_{\delta}\rho^{-\bm{\beta}}
sets of the form
\bigcup_{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}[T_{\rho}]}Y^{\prime}(T)
,
T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
.

(ii)

\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
is broad with error
\approx_{\delta}1
relative to the cover
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
.

Proof.

Using dyadic pigeonholing, we can select a number
\mu\geq 1
and a
(\log 1/\delta)^{-1}
refinement
(\mathbb{T},Y_{1})_{\delta}
of
(\mathbb{T},Y)
with the property that
\#\{T\in\mathbb{T}\colon x\in Y_{1}(T)\}\sim\mu
for each
x\in\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y_{1}(T)
. Apply Lemma
7.8
to each point
x\in\bigcup Y_{1}(T)
. We obtain a scale
\rho_{x}\in[\delta,1]
, and a set of disjoint
\rho_{x}
balls
\mathcal{B}_{x}
(each ball in
\mathcal{B}_{x}
is a subset of
S^{2}
). After further dyadic pigeonholing we can select the following:

•

A common scale
\rho
;

•

Multiplicities
\nu\geq 1
and
N\lesssim\rho^{-\bm{\beta}}
;

•

A set
\mathcal{B}
of
\rho
balls (each ball in
\mathcal{B}
is a subset of
S^{2}
), whose
100\rho
neighbourhoods are disjoint;

•

A
\gtrsim(\log 1/\delta)^{-1}
refinement
(\mathbb{T},Y_{2})_{\delta}
of
(\mathbb{T},Y_{1})_{\delta}
.

We can select the above numbers and sets so that the following property holds: for each
x\in\bigcup_{\mathbb{T}}Y_{2}(T)
, the set of unit vectors
\{\mathrm{dir}(T)\colon x\in Y_{2}(T)\}\subset S^{2}
can be covered by a union of
N
balls from
\mathcal{B}
, and for each such ball, we have that the set
B\cap\{\mathrm{dir}(T)\colon x\in Y_{2}(T)\}
has cardinality
\nu
and is broad with error
O(1)
inside
B
.

After refining
\mathbb{T}
by a factor of
\approx_{\delta}1
, we obtain a new pair
(\mathbb{T}_{2},Y_{2})_{\delta}
that is a
\approx_{\delta}1
refinement of
(\mathbb{T},Y_{2})_{\delta}
, and a set
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
of
\rho
tubes with the property that
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
is a balanced partitioning cover of
\mathbb{T}_{2}
, and furthermore the family of convex sets
\{3T_{\rho}\colon T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}\}
is a partitioning cover of
\mathbb{T}_{2}
. This means that for each
T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
,
\mathbb{T}_{2}[T_{\rho}]=\mathbb{T}_{2}[3T_{\rho}]
.

For each
T\in\mathbb{T}_{2}
with
T\subset T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
, define

Y_{3}(T)=\{x\in Y_{2}(T)\colon\#\{T^{\prime}\in\mathbb{T}_{2}[T_{\rho}]\colon x\in Y_{2}(T^{\prime})\}\geq\kappa_{0}\nu\}.

Since
(\mathbb{T}_{2},Y_{2})_{\delta}
is a
\approx_{\delta}1
refinement of
(\mathbb{T},Y_{2})_{\delta}
, if
\kappa_{0}>0
is chosen sufficiently small (depending on the implicit constant mentioned previously), then
(\mathbb{T}_{2},Y_{3})_{\delta}
is a
\approx_{\delta}1
refinement of
(\mathbb{T}_{2},Y_{2})_{\delta}
. Furthermore, for each point
x\in\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}[3T_{\rho}]}Y_{3}(T)=\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}[T_{\rho}]}Y_{3}(T)
, we have that the set of unit vectors
\{\mathrm{dir}(T)\colon T\in\mathbb{T}_{2}[3T_{\rho}]=\mathbb{T}_{2}[T_{\rho}],\ x\in Y_{3}(T)\}
is broad inside
B(\mathrm{dir}(T_{\rho}),2\rho)
with error
O(1)
, in the sense of Definition
7.6
.
We conclude that the pair
(\mathbb{T}_{2},Y_{3})_{\delta}
and
\{3T_{\rho},T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}\}
satisfy the conclusions of Corollary
7.10
(with
3\rho
in place of
\rho
).
∎

The next result says that every pair
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
of
\delta
tubes and their associated shading is either broad at
some
scale
\delta<\!\!<\rho<\!\!<1
, or else the tubes in
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
are almost disjoint.

Lemma 7.11
.

Let
\delta>0
and let
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
be a pair of
\delta
tubes and their associated shading. Then at least one of the following must occur:

(A)

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\bm{\omega}/2}\sum_{T\in\mathbb{T}}|Y(T)|.

(7.5)

(B)

There is a scale
\rho\in[\delta^{1-\bm{\omega}/100},\delta^{\bm{\omega}/100}],
a
\approx_{\delta}1
refinement
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
of
(\mathbb{T},Y)
, and a balanced partitioning cover
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
of
\mathbb{T}
, so that
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
is broad with error
O(1)
relative to the cover
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
.

Proof.

Let
r=\delta^{\bm{\omega}/100}
. After replacing
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
by a
\sim 1
refinement, we can find a partitioning cover
\mathbb{T}_{r}
of
\mathbb{T}
. Apply Corollary
7.10
to each set
(\mathbb{T}^{T_{r}},Y^{T_{r}})_{\delta/r}
. After dyadic pigeonholing
\mathbb{T}_{r}
and
\mathbb{T}
, we can suppose that the resulting scale, which we will call
\tilde{\rho}
, is the same for each
T_{r}\in\mathbb{T}_{r}
. Define
\rho=\tilde{\rho}r
, and let
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})
be the
\approx_{\delta}1
refinement of
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
consisting of the tubes and their associated shadings coming from the conclusion of Corollary
7.10
for each
T_{r}\in\mathbb{T}_{r}
.

Recall that the tubes
\mathbb{T}_{r}
form a partitioning cover of
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
, and for each
T_{r}\in\mathbb{T}_{r}
, the (re-scaled)
\rho
tubes coming from Corollary
7.10
form a partitioning cover of
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}[T_{r}]
. We conclude that if we define
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
to be the union of these
\rho
tubes, then
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
is a partitioning cover of
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
, and furthermore
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
is broad with error
O(1)
relative to the cover
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
.

At this point, we have constructed the pair
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
and
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
, which satisfies all of the requirements for Conclusion (B) of Lemma
7.11
with one exception — we know that
\rho\in[\delta,\delta^{\bm{\omega}/100}]
, but we do not know that
\rho\in[\delta^{1-\bm{\omega}/100},\delta^{\bm{\omega}/100}].
Our next task is to show that if
\rho<\delta^{1-\bm{\omega}/100}
, then Conclusion (A) holds.

Since the tubes in
\mathbb{T}_{r}
are essentially distinct,
O(r^{-2})
tubes can pass through a common point. By Conclusion (i) from Corollary
7.10
, we have that for each tube
T_{r}
and each
x\in T_{r}
we have

\#\{T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}[T_{r}]\colon x\in\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}^{\prime}[T_{\rho}]}Y^{\prime}(T)\}\lessapprox_{\delta}\rho^{-\bm{\beta}}\leq\delta^{-\bm{\omega}/100}.

Recall
\bm{\beta}=\bm{\omega}\bm{\zeta}/100
was fixed at the beginning of Section 7.1.

Finally, for each
T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
, at most
(\rho/\delta)^{2}
tubes from
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}[T_{\rho}]
can pass through a common point and at most
r^{-2}
distinct
T_{r}\in\mathbb{T}_{r}
can pass through a common point. We conclude that

\#\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{Y^{\prime}}(x)\lesssim\delta^{-\bm{\omega}/100}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\delta r}\Big)^{2}\quad\textrm{for each}\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}.

In summary, if
\rho<\delta^{1-\bm{\omega}/100}
, then Conclusion (A) holds. Otherwise, Conclusion (B) holds.
∎

We conclude this section with two results on the union of broad sets of vectors. The first is a straightforward result saying that a union of broad sets is broad. We omit the proof.

Lemma 7.12
.

Let
\delta>0
, let
B\subset S^{n-1}
be a disk, and let
\mathcal{V}_{i}
,
i=1,\ldots,N
be sets of unit vectors in
B
. Suppose that each set
\mathcal{V}_{i}
is broad with error
K
at scales
\geq\delta
inside
B
. Then the multi-set
\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{V}_{i}
is broad with error
K
at scales
\geq\delta
inside
B
.

The second result described how broadness combines across scales.

Lemma 7.13
.

Let
0<\delta<\rho\leq 1
. Let
\mathcal{U}
be a set of unit vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For each
u\in\mathcal{U}
, let
\mathcal{V}_{u}\subset B(u,\rho)\subset S^{n-1}
be contained in the disk of radius
\rho
centered at
u
.
Suppose that
\mathcal{U}
is broad with error
K_{1}
at scales
\geq\rho
, and that each set
\mathcal{V}_{u}
is broad with error
K_{2}
at scales
\geq\delta
inside
B(u,\rho)
.
Suppose furthermore that each set
\mathcal{V}_{u}
has comparable cardinality (up to a factor of 2).
Then the multi-set
\bigsqcup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\mathcal{V}_{u}
is broad with error
O(K_{1}K_{2})
at scales
\geq\delta
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{V}=\bigsqcup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\mathcal{V}_{u}
. By hypothesis, there is a number
M_{1}
so that
M_{1}\leq\#\mathcal{V}_{u}\leq 2M_{1}
for each set
\mathcal{V}_{u}
. Let
X=\sup_{u_{0}}\#\{u\in\mathcal{U}\colon\angle(u,u_{0})\leq\rho\}
