) to conclude that

\Big|N_{b\theta_{0}}(P_{0})\cap\bigcup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}Y(P)\Big|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{8\eta}|N_{b\theta}(P_{0})|.

(8.26)

Recall that (
8.26
) holds for each
P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{3}^{\prime}
, and
(\mathcal{P}_{3}^{\prime},Y_{3})_{a\times b\times c}
is a
\gtrapprox_{\delta}1
refinement of
(\mathcal{P},Y)_{a\times b\times c}
. After replacing
(\mathcal{P}_{3}^{\prime},Y_{3})_{a\times b\times c}
by a further
\sim 1
refinement
(\mathcal{P}_{3}^{\prime},Y_{3}^{\prime})_{a\times b\times c}
, we can suppose that for each
x\in\bigcup_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{3}^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}_{3}
, we have

\Big|N_{b\theta_{0}}(x)\cap\bigcup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}Y(P)\Big|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{8\eta}|N_{b\theta_{0}}(x)|.

Finally, if
(\mathbb{T}_{1},Y_{1}^{\prime})_{\delta}
is the refinement of
(\mathbb{T}_{1},Y_{1})_{\delta}
induced by
(\mathcal{P}_{3}^{\prime},Y_{3}^{\prime})_{a\times b\times c}
, then by Definition
7.1
, Item (ii),
(\mathbb{T}_{1},Y_{1}^{\prime})_{\delta}
is a
\approx_{\delta}1
-refinement of
(\mathbb{T}_{1},Y_{1})_{\delta}
, and for each

we have

\Big|N_{b\theta_{0}}(x)\cap\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}^{\prime}}Y(T)\Big|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{8\eta}|N_{b\theta_{0}}(x)|.

(8.27)

Since
b\theta_{0}\geq\delta^{-\varepsilon_{1}}a
, by Corollary
5.19
we see that Conclusion (A) holds, provided we select
\eta>0
sufficiently small depending on
\varepsilon_{1}
, and select
\alpha\leq\varepsilon_{1}\bm{\omega}/2
.

Henceforth we shall suppose that
\theta_{0}\leq\delta^{-\varepsilon_{1}}(a/b)
, i.e.

\sup_{x}\sup_{P,P^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{P}_{3})_{Y_{3}}(x)}\angle\big(\Pi(P),\ \Pi(P^{\prime})\big)\leq\delta^{-\varepsilon_{1}}(a/b).

(8.28)

Step 3.

Recall the discussion following Definition
7.3
(the quantity “
K
” in that discussion is
\delta^{-\varepsilon_{1}}
in this context); after replacing
(\mathcal{P}_{3},Y_{3})_{a\times b\times c}
by a
\sim 1
refinement, which we will denote by

refinement

, we can find a set

of pairwise distinct prisms of dimensions

\mathcal{P}_{4}
.

(b)

are disjoint.

Each

is a prism of dimensions

with the property that for each

(recall that

, and thus

).

Let

that maximizes

so in particular

. Let

; let

to
\mathcal{P}_{5}
. Then
(\mathcal{P}_{5},Y_{5})_{a\times b\times c}
is a
\gtrsim\delta^{3\varepsilon_{1}}
refinement of
(\mathcal{P}_{4},Y_{4})_{a\times b\times c}
, and we have the following analogue of Items (a) and (b).

(a
′
)

The sets

become a partition of
\mathcal{P}_{5}
.

(b
′
)

The sets
\big\{\bigcup_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{5}\langle Z\rangle}Y_{5}(P)\colon Z\in\mathcal{Z}\big\}
are disjoint.

For each
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
, the sets in

are convex sets of dimensions comparable to

, i.e. the sets are comparable to

After replacing

and

refinements, we may suppose that each set

).

Step 4.

For notational convenience, we will fix a prism
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
. In what follows, we will find certain quantities (for example certain scales, multiplicities, etc.), and navigate between different cases depending on the specifics of the arrangement
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{Z}
. However, by pigeonholing the set
\mathcal{Z}
, we may suppose that all quantities described below are the same (up to a factor of 2) for each
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
, and thus the same cases occur for each
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
.

Apply Proposition
4.6
(factoring convex sets) to
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{Z}
. We obtain a number
m\geq 1
; a
\approx_{\delta}1
refinement
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{Z}^{\prime}
of
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{Z}
, and a partitioning cover
\mathcal{W}_{Z}
of
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{Z}^{\prime}
consisting of congruent prisms; we shall denote the dimensions of these prisms by
s\times t\times 1
(since each prism contains at least one tube, we know that the longest dimension is
\sim 1
). We have that
\mathcal{W}_{Z}
factors
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{Z}^{\prime}
from below with respect to the Frostman Convex Wolff Axioms, and from above with respect to the Katz-Tao Convex Wolff Axioms, both with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}1
. Finally,

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{Z})\leq m,\quad\textrm{and}\quad\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{Z}^{\prime}[W]\approx_{\delta}m\frac{|W|}{|\tilde{T}|}\quad\ \textrm{for each}\ W\in\mathcal{W}_{Z}.

(8.29)

We first consider the case where

m\leq\delta^{-\bm{\zeta}}.

(8.30)

Our goal is to show that Conclusion (B) of Lemma
8.3
holds.

As described at the beginning of Step 4, we can suppose that (
8.30
) is true for at least half the prisms
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{6}\subset\mathcal{P}_{5}
be given by
\mathcal{P}_{6}=\bigcup_{Z}\phi_{Z}^{-1}(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{Z}^{\prime})
, where the union is taken over those prisms in
\mathcal{Z}
for which (
8.30
) holds (note that
\mathcal{P}_{6}\subset\mathcal{P}_{5}
, since each tube in
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{Z}^{\prime}\subset\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{Z}
is the image of a prism from
\mathcal{P}_{5}
under the map
\phi_{Z}
). Let
Y_{6}
be the restriction of
Y_{5}
to
\mathcal{P}_{6}
.

We have that
(\mathcal{P}_{6},Y_{6})_{a\times b\times c}
is a
\gtrapprox_{\delta}1
refinement of
(\mathcal{P}_{5},Y_{5})
. Undoing the scaling
\phi_{Z}
, we have that for each
P\in\mathcal{P}_{6}
,

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}\big(\mathcal{P}_{6}\langle 2\square(P)\rangle\big)\lesssim\sup_{Z\in\mathcal{Z}}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{P}_{6}\langle Z\rangle)\leq m.

We conclude that

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathcal{P}_{6})\lesssim m.

Applying a final dyadic pigeonholing, we can select a
\approx_{\delta}1
refinement
(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{a\times b\times c}
of
(\mathcal{P}_{6},Y_{6})_{a\times b\times c}
(this in turn induces a
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{3\varepsilon_{1}}
refinement
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
of
(\mathbb{T}_{4},Y_{4})_{\delta}
) and a set
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
so that the following holds:
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}^{\prime}
is a balanced partitioning cover of
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
, and
(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{a\times b\times c}
is a robustly
\approx_{\delta}\delta^{3\varepsilon_{1}}
-dense two-scale grains decomposition of
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
wrt
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{\rho}
.

Since
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathcal{P}^{\prime})\lesssim m
, Conclusion (B), Item (iv) of Lemma
8.3
is satisfied.
(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{a\times b\times c}
satisfies Conclusion (B), Item (iii) by construction.
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
satisfies Conclusion (B), Item (i), provided
\varepsilon_{1}
is chosen sufficiently small depending on
\varepsilon
. Finally, since

\#\big(\mathbb{T}^{\prime}[T_{\rho}]_{Y^{\prime}}(x)\big)\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{3\varepsilon_{1}}\#(\mathbb{T}[T_{\rho}])_{Y}(x)\quad\textrm{for every}\ T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}^{\prime}\ \textrm{and every}\ x\in\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}^{\prime}[T_{\rho}]}Y^{\prime}(T),

and since (by hypothesis)
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
is broad with error
\delta^{-\eta}
relative to
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
, we conclude that
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
is broad with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\eta-3\varepsilon_{1}}
relative to
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}^{\prime}
. We will select
\eta
and
\varepsilon_{1}
sufficiently small so that this quantity is
\leq\delta^{-\varepsilon}
. This verifies Conclusion (B), Item (ii)
3
3

3

To be precise, we can only ensure that the error is
\leq\delta^{-\varepsilon}
provided
\delta>0
is sufficiently small, depending on the implicit constant in the above
\gtrapprox
notation. However if this fails then Conclusion (A) holds, provided we select
\kappa>0
sufficiently small.
.

In summary, if (
8.30
) holds, then Conclusion (B) of Lemma
8.3
is satisfied. Henceforth we will suppose that (
8.30
) fails, i.e.

m>\delta^{-\bm{\zeta}}.

(8.31)

Step 5.

In Step 4, we fixed a choice of prism
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
. We will continue to fix this choice of
Z
, and in additional we will fix a choice of
W\in\mathcal{W}_{Z}
. As in Step 4, we can assume (by dyadic pigeonholing) that all relevant scales, multiplicities, etc. are approximately the same (up to a factor of 2) for each
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
and each
W\in\mathcal{W}_{Z}
).

In the arguments that follow, we will analyze the pairs
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{Z}[W],\tilde{Y}_{5})_{\rho}
constructed in Step 4. For notational convenience, we will refer to such a pair as
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}},\tilde{Y})_{\rho}
. Recall that this pair is
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\eta}
dense; the cardinality of
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}
is given by (
8.29
); and
m
satisfies (
8.31
). In particular, we have

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{W})\lessapprox_{\delta}1.

(8.32)

Apply Corollary
7.10
(finding a broad scale) to the set
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}
. Denote the “output” scale of this Corollary by
\tau
(in Corollary
7.10
, this output scale is called
\rho
, but that variable is already in use). Abusing notation, we will continue to use
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}},\tilde{Y})_{\rho}
to refer to the output of Corollary
7.10
. Thus there is a set
\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
that forms a balanced partitioning cover of
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}
, and
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}},\tilde{Y})_{\rho}
is broad with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}1
relative to
\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
. Furthermore,

\textrm{the sets}\ \bigcup_{\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[T_{\tau}]}\tilde{Y}(\tilde{T}),\quad T_{\tau}\in\mathbb{T}_{\tau}\quad\textrm{are}\ \lesssim\tau^{-\bm{\beta}}\ \textrm{overlapping}.

(8.33)

We claim that

\tau\geq\rho^{1-\bm{\omega}/8},

(8.34)

or else Conclusion (A) of Lemma
8.3
holds, and we are done. To verify this claim, note that if (
8.34
) failed, then the sets
\{\tilde{Y}(\tilde{T})\colon\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}\}
are
\lesssim\rho^{-\bm{\omega}/4}\tau^{-\bm{\beta}}\leq\delta^{-\bm{\omega}/2}
overlapping; but this fact, combined with Item (b
′
) from Step 3, gives Conclusion (A) of Lemma
8.3
.

Step 6.

We would like to apply the estimate
\mathcal{E}(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\omega})
to each set
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T_{\tau}},\tilde{Y}^{T_{\tau}})_{\rho/\tau}
. However, we do not currently have a good estimate for
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T_{\tau}})
. To fix this problem, apply Proposition
4.8
(factoring convex sets with respect to the Frostman Slab Wolff Axioms) to each set
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[T_{\tau}],\tilde{Y})_{\rho}
, with
\varepsilon_{2}
in place of
\varepsilon
. We can do so, provided
\varepsilon_{1}
and
\eta
is selected sufficiently small compared to
\varepsilon_{2}
. This gives us a
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\varepsilon_{2}}
refinement
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}[T_{\tau}],\tilde{Y}^{\prime})_{\rho}
of
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[T_{\tau}],\tilde{Y})_{\rho}
, and a family of convex subsets of
T_{\tau}
, which we denote by
\mathcal{V}_{T_{\tau}}
, that factors
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}[T_{\tau}]
from below with respect to the Frostman Slab Wolff Axioms with error
\delta^{-\varepsilon_{2}}
. In addition,

\textrm{the sets}\ \bigcup_{\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}[V]}\tilde{Y}^{\prime}(\tilde{T}),\quad V\in\mathcal{V}_{T_{\tau}}\quad\textrm{are disjoint}.

(8.35)

After pigeonholing, we may suppose that the sets in
\mathcal{V}_{T_{\tau}}
have the same dimensions, and furthermore these dimensions are common across all
T_{\tau}\in\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
. Denote these dimensions
\theta\times\tau^{\prime}\times 1
. Since
V\subset T_{\tau}
, we must have
\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau
. We claim that this inequality is almost tight, in the sense that

\delta^{\varepsilon_{2}/\bm{\beta}}\tau\lessapprox_{\delta}\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau.

(8.36)

To verify this claim, note that
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}},\tilde{Y})_{\rho}
is broad with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}1
relative to the cover
\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
. Since
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}[T_{\tau}],\tilde{Y}^{\prime})_{\rho}
is a
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\varepsilon_{2}}
refinement of
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[T_{\tau}],\tilde{Y})_{\rho}
, by pigeonholing there is at least one point
x
for which the tubes in
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{Y^{\prime}}(x)
point in directions that are broad with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\varepsilon_{2}}
inside a cap of radius
\tau
. This means that there are at least two tubes from this set that make an angle
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\varepsilon_{2}/\bm{\beta}}\tau
. On the other hand, by (
8.35
) we have that the pair of tubes described above must be contained in a common
\theta\times\tau^{\prime}\times 1
prism. This establishes (
8.36
).

Let
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
be the union of the sets
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}[T_{\tau}]
, as
T_{\tau}
ranges over the elements of
\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
. Let
\tilde{Y}^{\prime}
be the associated shading on
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
coming from the pairs
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}[T_{\tau}],\tilde{Y}^{\prime})_{\rho}
. Abusing notation, we will rename this pair
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}},\tilde{Y})_{\rho}
. At this point in the argument, this pair is
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\eta+\varepsilon_{2}}\geq\delta^{2\varepsilon_{2}}
dense.

Step 7.

We first consider the case where the prisms
\mathcal{V}_{T_{\tau}}
from Step 6 are almost tubes, in the sense that

\theta\geq\delta^{\bm{\zeta}/10}\tau^{\prime}.

(8.37)

If (
8.37
) is true, then each set
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{V}
consists of prisms of dimensions
\frac{\rho}{\tau^{\prime}}\times\frac{\rho}{\theta}\times 1
. The pair
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{V},\tilde{Y}^{V})_{\rho/\tau^{\prime}\times\rho/\theta\times 1}
is
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{2\varepsilon_{2}}
dense. Let us suppose for the moment that

\rho\leq\delta^{\sqrt{\varepsilon}_{2}}.

(8.38)

If we select
\varepsilon_{2}
sufficiently small so that
\varepsilon_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}_{2}\bm{\beta}\bm{\omega}
, and hence
\frac{8\varepsilon_{2}\bm{\beta}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}_{2}\bm{\beta}\bm{\omega}-8\varepsilon_{2}}\leq\frac{16\sqrt{\varepsilon}_{2}}{\bm{\omega}}
, then by (
8.34
) and (
8.36
), we have
\delta^{2\varepsilon_{2}}\geq\big(\frac{\rho}{\tau^{\prime}}\big)^{\frac{32\sqrt{\varepsilon_{2}}}{\bm{\omega}}}
.

Thus if
\varepsilon_{2}
is chosen sufficiently small compared to
\varepsilon_{3}
and
\bm{\omega}
, and if (
8.38
) is true, then we can apply the estimate
\mathcal{F}(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\omega})
(recall Definition
5.4
and Remark
5.6
, Situation 3) to estimate the volume of each (rescaled) set
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{V},\tilde{Y}^{V})_{\rho/\tau^{\prime}\times\rho/\theta\times 1}
, with
\varepsilon_{3}
in place of
\varepsilon
and
32\sqrt{\varepsilon_{2}}/\bm{\omega}
in place of
\eta
. This gives the estimate

\Big|\bigcup_{\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V]}\tilde{Y}^{V}(\tilde{T}^{V})\Big|\gtrsim\delta^{\varepsilon_{3}}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}}\tilde{m}^{-1}(\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V])|\tilde{T}^{V}|\Big(\tilde{m}^{-3/2}\tilde{\ell}(\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V])|\tilde{T}^{V}|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\bm{\sigma}},

(8.39)

where we define

\tilde{m}=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V])\leq C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\tilde{\mathbb{T}})\leq m\quad\textrm{and}\quad\tilde{\ell}=C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V])\lesssim\delta^{-\varepsilon_{2}}.

(8.40)

(for the first inequality, we used (
8.29
)). On the other hand, if (
8.38
) is false, then (
8.39
) follows from the fact that the LHS of (
8.39
) is bounded below by the volume of a single shading
|\tilde{Y}^{V}(T^{V})|
, and we can select a tube with volume

|\tilde{Y}^{V}(T^{V})|\gtrapprox\delta^{2\varepsilon_{2}}|T^{V}|=\delta^{2\varepsilon_{2}}\frac{\rho^{2}}{\tau^{\prime}\theta}\geq\delta^{2\varepsilon_{2}}\rho^{2}\geq\delta^{3\varepsilon_{2}}\geq\delta^{\varepsilon_{3}}.

(8.41)

Since
\sigma\in(0,2/3]
, by Remark
5.5
and Remark
5.6
, Situation 3,

\tilde{m}^{-1}(\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V])|\tilde{T}^{V}|\Big(\tilde{m}^{-3/2}\tilde{\ell}(\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V])|\tilde{T}^{V}|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\bm{\sigma}}\lesssim 1.

(8.42)

Combining (
8.41
) and (
8.42
) establishes (
8.39
) in the case where (
8.38
) is false. We conclude that (
8.39
) holds, independently of whether (
8.38
) is true or false.

Undoing the scaling
\phi_{V}
and substituting the values of
\tilde{m}
and
\tilde{\ell}
from (
8.40
), we have

\Big|\bigcup_{\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V]}\tilde{Y}(\tilde{T})\Big|\gtrsim\delta^{2\varepsilon_{3}}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}}m^{-1}(\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V])|\tilde{T}|\Big(m^{-3/2}(\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V])\frac{|\tilde{T}|^{1/2}}{|V|^{1/2}}\Big)^{-\bm{\sigma}}.

(8.43)

By (
8.33
), (
8.35
) (recall that we have renamed
\tilde{Y}^{\prime}
as
\tilde{Y}
), and (
8.43
), we have

\begin{split}\Big|\bigcup_{\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}}\tilde{Y}(\tilde{T})\Big|&\gtrsim\tau^{\bm{\beta}}\sum_{T_{\tau}\in\mathbb{T}_{\tau}}\sum_{V\in\mathcal{V}_{T_{\tau}}}\Big|\bigcup_{\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V]}\tilde{Y}(\tilde{T})\Big|\\
&\gtrsim\delta^{2\varepsilon_{3}+\bm{\beta}}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}}m^{-1}(\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}})|\tilde{T}|\Big(m^{-3/2}(\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V])\frac{|\tilde{T}|^{1/2}}{|V|^{1/2}}\Big)^{-\bm{\sigma}}.\end{split}

(8.44)

Next, by (
8.32
) and using the fact that
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}[T_{\tau}],Y^{\prime})_{\rho}
is a
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\varepsilon_{2}}
refinement of
(\mathbb{T}[T_{\tau}],\tilde{Y})_{\rho}
, we have that
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{W})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{2}}
. By (
8.29
),
\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}}\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{2\varepsilon_{2}}\Big(m\frac{|W|}{|\tilde{T}|}\Big)
. Since

\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V]\leq C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{W})\frac{|V|}{|W|}(\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{2}}\frac{|V|}{|W|}\Big(m\frac{|W|}{|\tilde{T}|}\Big),

using (
8.31
), (
8.36
), (
8.37
), and (
8.44
) we conclude that

\begin{split}\Big|\bigcup_{\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}}\tilde{Y}(\tilde{T})\Big|&\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{2\varepsilon_{3}+\bm{\beta}-\bm{\zeta}\bm{\sigma}/2}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}}m^{-1}(m\frac{|W|}{|\tilde{T}|})|\tilde{T}|\Big(m^{-1}\big(m\frac{|V|}{|\tilde{T}|}\big)\frac{|\tilde{T}|^{1/2}}{|V|^{1/2}}\Big)^{-\bm{\sigma}}\\
&\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{2\varepsilon_{3}+\bm{\beta}-\bm{\zeta}\bm{\sigma}/4}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}+\bm{\sigma}}|W|.\end{split}

(8.45)

Step 8.

Let us analyze (
8.45
). First, the set on the LHS of (
8.45
) is contained in
W
, which is a prism of dimensions
s\times t\times 1
. Second, the quantities
\varepsilon_{3}
and
\bm{\beta}
are chosen after
\bm{\zeta}
and
\bm{\sigma}
, so we can select the former quantities to ensure that
\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{3}+\bm{\beta}-\bm{\zeta}\bm{\sigma}/4}\geq\delta^{-\bm{\zeta}\bm{\sigma}/5}
.

Thus by pigeonholing, we can select a ball
B
of radius
\theta
(recall that
\rho\leq\theta\leq s
) with the property that

\Big|B\cap\bigcup_{\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}}\tilde{Y}(T)\Big|\gtrapprox\delta^{-\bm{\zeta}\bm{\sigma}/5}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}+\bm{\sigma}}|B|.

(8.46)

Recall that at the beginning of Step 4 we fixed a prism
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
. The set of
\rho
tubes
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}
are the images of prisms from
\mathcal{P}_{3}\langle Z\rangle
under the linear map
\phi_{Z}
. Let
B^{\dagger}=\phi_{Z}^{-1}(B)
, where
B
is the ball described above. Then
B^{\dagger}
is an ellipsoid of dimensions
\theta\frac{a}{\rho}\times\theta c\times\theta c
. By (
8.46
), we have

\Big|B^{\dagger}\cap\bigcup_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{1}}Y_{1}(P)\Big|\gtrapprox\delta^{-\bm{\zeta}\bm{\sigma}/5}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}+\bm{\sigma}}|B^{\dagger}|.

(8.47)

Let
1\leq i\leq\varepsilon_{1}^{-1}
be the index so that
\delta^{i\varepsilon_{1}}\leq\theta\frac{a}{\rho}<\delta^{(i-1)\varepsilon_{1}}
. Such an index exists since
\theta\in[\rho,1]
and
\delta\leq\frac{a}{\rho}=\frac{ac}{b}\leq c\leq 1
. Recall that we defined
\tau_{i}=\delta^{i\varepsilon_{1}}
, so
\theta a/\rho\leq\tau_{i}\delta^{-\varepsilon_{1}}
implies
\rho/\theta\geq\delta^{\varepsilon_{1}}a/\tau_{i}\geq\delta^{1+\varepsilon_{1}}/\tau_{i}
. Then there exists a ball
B_{\tau_{i}}
of radius
\tau_{i}
, so that

\lambda_{i}\geq\Big|B_{\tau_{i}}\cap\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}}Y_{1}(T)\Big||B_{\tau_{i}}|^{-1}\geq\delta^{3\varepsilon_{1}-\bm{\zeta}\bm{\sigma}/5}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}+\bm{\sigma}}\geq\delta^{4\varepsilon_{1}-\bm{\zeta}\bm{\sigma}/5}\Big(\frac{\delta}{\tau_{i}}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}}\frac{|T|^{\bm{\sigma}/2}}{|T_{\tau_{i}}|^{\bm{\sigma}/2}}.

(8.48)

Combining (
8.25
) and (
8.48
), we conclude that Conclusion (A) of Lemma
8.3
holds, provided we select
\varepsilon_{1}\leq\frac{1}{100}\bm{\zeta}\bm{\sigma}
and select
\alpha
sufficiently small.

This concludes our analysis of the case where (
8.37
) holds (the analysis of this case began at the start of Step 7). Henceforth we shall suppose that (
8.37
) fails.

Step 9.

We shall now return to the start of Step 7, except, instead of assuming (
8.37
), we will instead suppose that

\theta<\delta^{\bm{\zeta}/10}\tau^{\prime}.

(8.49)

Informally, (
8.49
) says that the prisms
V\in\mathcal{V}_{T_{\tau}}
are flat.

Recall that in Steps 4 and 5, we fixed a prism
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
and a prism
W\in\mathcal{W}_{Z}
. In this step, we will fix a
\tau
tube
T_{\tau}\in\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
and a
\theta\times\tau^{\prime}\times 1
prism
V\in\mathcal{V}_{T_{\tau}}
. Define
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}=\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V]
and let
Y^{\dagger}
be the restriction of
\tilde{Y}
to
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}
. Thus
(\mathbb{T}^{\dagger},Y^{\dagger})_{\rho}
is a set of
\rho
tubes contained in
V
, and

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}((\mathbb{T}^{\dagger})^{V})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\varepsilon_{2}}.

(8.50)

After pigeonholing, we may suppose that
(\mathbb{T}^{\dagger},Y^{\dagger})_{\rho}
is
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{2\varepsilon_{2}}
dense.
For each “stem”
T^{\dagger}_{0}\in\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}
, define the “hairbrush”

\mathcal{H}(T^{\dagger}_{0})=\{T^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\colon Y^{\dagger}(T_{0}^{{\dagger}})\cap Y^{\dagger}(T^{{\dagger}})\neq\emptyset\}.

We claim that for each tube
T^{\dagger}_{0}\in\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}
, the set

N_{(\tau^{\prime}/\theta)\rho}(T^{\dagger}_{0})\cap\bigcup_{T^{{\dagger}}\in\mathcal{H}(T^{\dagger}_{0})}Y^{\dagger}(T^{\dagger})

(8.51)

is contained in a rectangular prism of dimensions comparable to
\rho\times(\tau^{\prime}/\theta)\rho\times 1
; we will call this rectangular prism
X=X(T^{{\dagger}}_{0})
. This claim follows from straightforward geometric considerations — See Figure
13
. Define
Y(X)
to be the set (
8.51
), so
Y(X)\subset X
.

[formula]
[formula]
[formula]

Figure 13:
All tubes (red) in this figure intersect the stem tube
T^{\dagger}_{0}
(black). Since each tube is contained in the
\theta\times\tau^{\prime}\times 1
prism
V
, each tube makes angle
\lesssim\theta/\tau^{\prime}
with the plane
\Pi(V)
. Since each red tube intersect
T^{\dagger}_{0}
, the union of these tubes, intersected with the
(\tau^{\prime}/\theta)\rho
neighbourhood of
T^{\dagger}_{0}
, are contained in the prism
X=X(T^{{\dagger}}_{0})
(green) of dimensions
\frac{\theta}{\tau^{\prime}}\cdot(\frac{\tau^{\prime}}{\theta})\rho\times(\frac{\tau^{\prime}}{\theta})\rho\times 1=\rho\times(\frac{\tau^{\prime}}{\theta})\rho\times 1
.

Next, we claim that if
\varepsilon_{2}
is chosen sufficiently small compared to
\varepsilon_{3}
, then for a
\gtrapprox_{\delta}1
fraction of the tubes
T^{\dagger}_{0}\in\mathbb{T}^{{\dagger}}
we have

|Y(X)|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\varepsilon_{3}}|X|,\quad\textrm{where}\ X=X(T^{\dagger}_{0}).

(8.52)

The estimate (
8.52
) says that the shading
Y(X)
is
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\varepsilon_{3}}
dense. This is a standard Cordoba-type
L^{2}
argument. In brief, let
Y^{\ddagger}(T^{\dagger})\subset Y^{\dagger}(T^{\dagger}),\ T^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}
be a regular shading, in the sense of Definition
5.8
, with
|Y^{\ddagger}(T^{\dagger})|\geq\frac{1}{2}|Y^{\dagger}(T^{\dagger})|
. By pigeonholing, a
\gtrapprox_{\delta}1
fraction of the tubes
T^{\dagger}_{0}\in\mathbb{T}^{{\dagger}}
satisfy

|\{x\in Y^{\dagger}(T^{\dagger}_{0})\colon\#\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{Y^{\ddagger}}(x)\geq\frac{1}{4}\#\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{Y^{\dagger}}(x)\}|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{2\varepsilon_{1}}|T^{\dagger}_{0}|.

(8.53)

For each point
x
in the set on the LHS of (
8.53
), we can select a tube
T^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}
with
x\in Y^{{\ddagger}}(T)
and
\angle(\mathrm{dir}(T^{\dagger}_{0}),\mathrm{dir}(T^{\dagger}))\gtrapprox_{\delta}\tau
(recall that in Step 5, we refined our pair
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}},\tilde{Y})_{\rho}
to be broad with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}1
relative to
\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
). We now choose a
\delta/\tau
-separated set of points from the LHS of (
8.53
), and consider the corresponding tubes
\{T^{\dagger}\}
. Since each shading
Y^{{\ddagger}}(T^{\dagger})
is regular, we have that the sum of the volumes of these shadings, restricted to
X
, has volume
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{2\varepsilon_{1}}|X|
:

\sum_{\{T^{{\dagger}}\}}|Y^{{\ddagger}}(T^{{\dagger}})\cap X|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{2\varepsilon_{1}}|X|.

Finally, we use a Cordoba-style
L^{2}
argument to show that the corresponding shadings
\{Y^{{\ddagger}}(T)\}
are almost disjoint inside
X
; this gives (
8.52
).

Abusing notation, we will refine
\mathbb{T}^{{\dagger}}
so that each
T^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}
satisfies (
8.52
). By dyadic pigeonholing and replacing
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}
by a
\approx_{\delta}1
refinement (abusing notation, we will continue to refer to this set as
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}
), we can select a number
M
and a set
\mathcal{X}
of essentially distinct
\rho\times\frac{\tau^{\prime}\rho}{\theta}\times 1
prisms of cardinality
\#\mathcal{X}=M^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T}^{\dagger})
, so that

(a)

For each
X\in\mathcal{X}
, there are
\sim M
tubes
T^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}
with
T^{\dagger}\subset X
and
X(T^{\dagger})
comparable to
X
. Denote this latter set by
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{X}
.

(b)

Each set
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}[X],\ X\in\mathcal{X}
has the same cardinality (up to a factor of 2).

Note that
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{X}\subset\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}[X]
, but the two sets need not be equal; it could be the case that
\#\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}[X]
is much larger than
\#\mathcal{X}
. In particular,
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}=\bigsqcup_{X\in\mathcal{X}}\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{X}
, but
\mathcal{X}
might not be a partitioning cover of
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}
. For example, there could exist a tube in
\mathbb{T}^{{\dagger}}[X]
that is contained in a different prism
V^{\prime}
; such a tube will be also be contained in the set
\mathbb{T}^{{\dagger}}_{X^{\prime}},
where
X^{\prime}
a prism with orientation compatible with
V^{\prime}
(recall Figure
13
). In particular, it is possible that
X
and
X^{\prime}
intersect transversely.

Step 10.

In Step 9 we fixed a choice of
\tau
tube
T_{\tau}\in\mathbb{T}_{\tau}
and a
\theta\times\tau^{\prime}\times 1
prism
V\in\mathcal{V}_{T_{\tau}}
. We then constructed a pair
(\mathcal{X},Y)_{\rho\times\frac{\tau^{\prime}}{\theta}\rho\times 1}
. The set
\mathcal{X}
depended on the choice of prism
V\in\mathcal{V}_{T_{\tau}}
; we will highlight this dependence by writing
\mathcal{X}_{V}
. In this step we will analyze the interaction between different collections
\mathcal{X}_{V}
.

Define
\mathcal{V}=\bigcup_{T_{\tau}\in\mathbb{T}_{\tau}}\mathcal{V}_{T_{\tau}}
. Note that
\mathcal{V}
is a set of
\theta\times\tau^{\prime}\times 1
prisms contained in
W
(the set
W
was fixed at Step 5). The quantity
M
from Step 9 depends on the choice of
V
, but after pigeonholing and refining
\mathcal{V}
we may suppose that this number is the same (up to a factor of 2) for every
V\in\mathcal{V}
.

We will first consider the case where

\#\mathbb{T}^{{\dagger}}_{X}\sim M\leq\delta^{-\bm{\zeta}/100}\frac{|X|}{|T^{\dagger}|}.

(8.54)

We will show that Conclusion (A) of Lemma
8.3
holds.

Recall from Figure
13
that the prisms
X\in\mathcal{X}_{V}
and
V
have compatible orientations, in the sense that
X^{V}
is a prism of dimensions comparable to
\frac{\rho}{\theta}\times\frac{\rho}{\theta}\times 1
. Thus we will refer to the set
(\mathcal{X}_{V}^{V},Y^{V})_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}\times\frac{\rho}{\theta}\times 1})
as
(\mathbb{T}_{\frac{\rho}{\theta},V},Y_{V})_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}
.

We claim that

\textrm{the sets}\ \Big\{\ \bigcup_{X\in\mathcal{X}_{V}}Y(X),\ V\in\mathcal{V}\Big\}\quad\textrm{are}\ \leq\delta^{-\bm{\beta}}\ \textrm{overlapping}.

(8.55)

This claim follows from combining (
8.33
) and (
8.35
) (and noting that
\tau^{-\bm{\beta}}\leq\delta^{-\bm{\beta}}
).

Observe that for each
V\in\mathcal{V}
, we have

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\frac{\rho}{\theta},V})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{2}}.

(8.56)

This is because
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{V})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{2}}
(recall that
\mathcal{V}
factors
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}
from below with small error with respect to the Frostman Slab Wolff Axioms), and by Item (b) from Step 9, each
X\in\mathcal{X}_{V}
contains the same number (up to a factor of 2) of tubes from
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V]
. This means that
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathcal{X}_{V}^{V})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{2}}
, which is precisely (
8.56
).

Define

m_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}=mM^{-1}\frac{|X|}{|\tilde{T}|},

(8.57)

where
m
is as defined in (
8.29
), and
|X|=\rho\times\frac{\tau^{\prime}}{\theta}\rho\times 1=\frac{\tau^{\prime}}{\theta}\rho^{2}
is the volume of a prism from
\mathcal{X}_{V}
. We have

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\frac{\rho}{\theta},V})\lessapprox_{\delta}mM^{-1}\frac{|X|}{|\tilde{T}|}\leq m_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}.

(8.58)

Finally, we compute

\sum_{V\in\mathcal{V}}(\#\mathbb{T}_{\frac{\rho}{\theta},V})\gtrapprox_{\delta}M^{-1}\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[W]\gtrapprox mM^{-1}\frac{|W|}{|\tilde{T}|},

(8.59)

where
W
is the prism fixed at Step 5, and the final inequality used (
8.29
).

Fix a choice of
V\in\mathcal{V}
. Applying the estimate
\mathcal{E}(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\omega})
to
(\mathbb{T}_{\frac{\rho}{\theta},V},Y_{V})_{\rho/\theta}
with
\varepsilon_{4}
in place of
\varepsilon
and using (
8.56
), we have

\begin{split}\Big|\bigcup_{T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}\in\mathbb{T}_{\frac{\rho}{\theta},V}}Y_{V}(T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}})\Big|&\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\varepsilon_{4}+2\varepsilon_{2}}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}}m_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}^{-1}(\#\mathbb{T}_{\frac{\rho}{\theta},V})|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|\Big(m_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}^{-3/2}(\#\mathbb{T}_{\frac{\rho}{\theta},V})|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\bm{\sigma}}.\end{split}

(8.60)

In the above inequality, we used (
8.58
) plus the fact that
\bm{\sigma}\leq 2/3
(the latter inequality allows us to replace
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\frac{\rho}{\theta},V})
with the potentially larger quantity
m_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}
). Undoing the scaling
\phi_{V}
and using (
8.55
), we conclude that

\begin{split}\Big|\bigcup_{\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}}Y(\tilde{T})\Big|&\geq\Big|\bigcup_{V\in\mathcal{V}}\bigcup_{X\in\mathcal{X}_{V}}Y(X)\Big|\\
&\geq\delta^{\bm{\beta}}\sum_{V\in\mathcal{V}}\Big|\bigcup_{X\in\mathcal{X}_{V}}Y(X)\Big|\\
&\geq\delta^{\bm{\beta}}\frac{|X|}{|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|}\sum_{V\in\mathcal{V}}\Big|\bigcup_{T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}\in\mathbb{T}_{\frac{\rho}{\theta},V}}Y_{V}(T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}})\Big|\\
&\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\bm{\beta}}\frac{|X|}{|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|}\cdot\delta^{\varepsilon_{4}+2\varepsilon_{2}}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}}m_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}^{-1}\Big(\sum_{V\in\mathcal{V}}\#\mathbb{T}_{\frac{\rho}{\theta},V}\Big)|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|\Big(m_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}^{-3/2}\big(\sup_{V\in\mathcal{V}}\#\mathbb{T}_{\frac{\rho}{\theta},V}\big)|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\bm{\sigma}}\\
&\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\bm{\beta}+2\varepsilon_{4}}\frac{|X|}{|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|}\cdot\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}}m_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}^{-1}\Big(mM^{-1}\frac{|W|}{|\tilde{T}|}\Big)|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|\Big(m_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}^{-3/2}\big(\sup_{V\in\mathcal{V}}\#\mathbb{T}_{\frac{\rho}{\theta},V}\big)|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\bm{\sigma}},\end{split}

(8.61)

where the final inequality used (
8.59
). Substituting (
8.57
) and simplifying, we obtain

\begin{split}\textrm{LHS}\ \eqref{boundInsideWZ}&\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\bm{\beta}+2\varepsilon_{4}}\frac{|X|}{|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|}\cdot\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}}\Big(mM^{-1}\frac{|X|}{|\tilde{T}|}\Big)^{-1}\Big(mM^{-1}\frac{|W|}{|\tilde{T}|}\Big)|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\Big(mM^{-1}\frac{|X|}{|\tilde{T}|}\Big)^{-3/2}\big(\sup_{V\in\mathcal{V}}\#\mathbb{T}_{\frac{\rho}{\theta},V}\big)|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\bm{\sigma}}\\
&\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\bm{\beta}+2\varepsilon_{4}}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}}|W|\Big(mM^{-1}\frac{|X|}{|\tilde{T}|}\Big)^{\bm{\sigma}/2}\Big(\Big(mM^{-1}\frac{|X|}{|\tilde{T}|}\Big)^{-1}\big(M^{-1}(\sup_{V\in\mathcal{V}}\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V])\big)|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\bm{\sigma}}\\
&\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\bm{\sigma}\bm{\zeta}/4}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}}|W|\Big(m^{-1}\frac{|\tilde{T}|}{|X|}\big(\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V]\big)|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\bm{\sigma}},\end{split}

(8.62)

where the second inequality used the fact that
\#\mathbb{T}_{\frac{\rho}{\theta},V}\sim M^{-1}(\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V])
for each
V\in\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{V}
, and the third inequality used (
8.31
), (
8.54
),
|T^{{\dagger}}|=|\tilde{T}|
, and the fact that
\bm{\beta}
and
\varepsilon_{4}
are small compared to
\bm{\sigma}\bm{\zeta}
.

Observe that the set on the LHS of (
8.62
) is contained in
|W|
, while the RHS involves the term
|W|
. Thus (
8.62
) gives a lower bound for the density of
\bigcup_{\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}}\tilde{Y}(\tilde{T})
inside
W
. This bound contains the term
\delta^{-\bm{\sigma}\bm{\zeta}/4}
— this quantity is much larger than 1, and this will eventually allow us to conclude that Conclusion (A) of Lemma
8.3
holds.

Let us analyze the final term in brackets
(\cdots)^{-\bm{\sigma}}
. We have

\begin{split}m^{-1}\frac{|\tilde{T}|}{|X|}\big(\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V]\big)|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|^{1/2}&\leq m^{-1}\frac{|\tilde{T}|}{|X|}\Big(C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{W})\frac{|V|}{|W|}(\#\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[W])\Big)|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|^{1/2}\\
&\lessapprox_{\delta}m^{-1}\frac{|\tilde{T}|}{|X|}\Big(\frac{|V|}{|W|}(m\frac{|W|}{|\tilde{T}|})\Big)|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|^{1/2}\\
&=\frac{|V|}{|X|}|T_{\frac{\rho}{\theta}}|^{1/2}\\
&=\frac{\theta}{\rho},\end{split}

(8.63)

where the second inequality used (
8.32
) and (
8.29
).

Combining (
8.62
) and (
8.63
), we conclude that

\Big|\bigcup_{\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}}Y(\tilde{T})\Big|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\bm{\sigma}\bm{\zeta}/4}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}+\bm{\sigma}}|W|.

(8.64)

Step 11.

We can now argue similarly to our reasoning in Step 8. The set on the LHS of (
8.64
) is contained in
W
, which is a prism of dimensions
s\times t\times 1
, with
\delta\leq\rho\leq\theta\leq s\leq 1
. Thus there exists a ball
B_{\theta}
of radius
\theta
so that

\Big|B_{\theta}\cap\bigcup_{\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}}Y(\tilde{T})\Big|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\bm{\sigma}\bm{\zeta}/4}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}+\bm{\sigma}}|B_{\theta}|.

Recall that at the beginning of Step 4 we fixed a prism
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
. The set of
\rho
tubes
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}
are the images of prisms from
\mathcal{P}_{3}\langle Z\rangle
under the linear map
\phi_{Z}
. Let
B^{\dagger}=\phi_{Z}^{-1}(B_{\theta})
;
B^{\dagger}
is an ellipsoid of dimensions
\theta\frac{a}{\rho}\times\theta c\times\theta c
, which satisfies

\Big|B^{\dagger}\cap\bigcup_{T_{1}\in\mathbb{T}_{1}}Y_{1}(T)\Big|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\bm{\sigma}\bm{\zeta}/4}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\Big)^{\bm{\omega}+\bm{\sigma}}|B^{\dagger}|.

This is the analogue of (
8.47
). An identical argument (in particular, note that
a\in[\delta,1]
) shows that Conclusion (A) of Lemma
8.3
holds, provided we select
\alpha<\bm{\sigma}\bm{\zeta}/10
. This concludes our analysis of the case where (
8.54
) holds.

Step 12
.
We shall now return to the start of Step 10, except, instead of assuming (
8.54
), we will instead suppose that

M>\delta^{-\bm{\zeta}/100}\frac{|X|}{|\tilde{T}|}.

(8.65)

We summarize the situation thus far:

•

We have fixed a choice of
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
(these are prisms of dimensions
\frac{a}{\rho}\times c\times c
) and
W\in\mathcal{W}_{Z}
.

•

We have a set
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}
of
\rho
tubes contained in
W
.

•

We have a set
\mathcal{V}
of
\theta\times\tau^{\prime}\times 1
prisms contained in
W
.

•

For each
V\in\mathcal{V}
, we have a set
\mathcal{X}_{V}
of
\rho\times\frac{\tau^{\prime}}{\theta}\rho\times 1
prisms, and a partition
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V]=\bigsqcup_{X\in\mathcal{X}_{V}}(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V])_{X}
.

•

Each set
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V])_{X}
has cardinality roughly
M
, where
M
satisfies (
8.65
).

•

We have a shading
\tilde{Y}
on
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}
so that
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}},\tilde{Y})_{\rho}
is
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{2\varepsilon_{2}}
dense.

Let
\mathcal{X}=\bigcup_{V\in\mathcal{V}}\mathcal{X}_{V}
(this is a slight abuse of notation, since in Step 9 we defined
\mathcal{X}
to be a set of the form
\mathcal{X}_{V}
, where the prism
V
was fixed in advance). To simplify notation, define
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{X}=(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}[V])_{X}
, where
V
is the (unique) prism for which
X\in\mathcal{X}_{V}
. Since
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}},\tilde{Y})_{\rho}
is
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{2\varepsilon_{2}}
dense, after a harmless refinement of
\mathcal{X}
we may suppose that each pair
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{X},\tilde{Y})_{\rho}
is
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{2\varepsilon_{2}}
dense, where
\tilde{Y}
is the restriction of the shading on
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}
to the set
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{X}
.

Apply Corollary
7.10
(finding a broad scale) to each pair
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{X},\tilde{Y})_{\rho}
, for each
X\in\mathcal{X}
. This yields a scale
\tilde{\rho}
and a
\gtrapprox_{\delta}1
refinement
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{X}^{\prime},\tilde{Y}^{\prime})_{\rho}
that is broad with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}1
with regard to a balanced partitioning cover of
\tilde{\rho}
tubes.

After dyadic pigeonholing and refining the set
\mathcal{X}
, we can suppose that the scale
\tilde{\rho}
from Corollary
7.10
is the same for each prism
X
. We claim that

\tilde{\rho}\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\bm{\zeta}/200}\rho.

(8.66)

To verify this claim, observe that for each
X\in\mathcal{X}
, the sets
\{\tilde{Y}^{\prime}(\tilde{T})\colon\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{X}^{\prime}\}
are
\lessapprox_{\delta}(\tau^{\prime}/\tilde{\rho})^{\bm{\beta}}(\tilde{\rho}/\rho)
overlapping. This is because the tubes from
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{X}
whose shadings pass through a common point
x
must point in directions confined to a
\rho\times\tau^{\prime}
region in the unit sphere
S^{2}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
(we identify
S^{2}
with the set of directions of tubes in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
). Since these tubes are essentially distinct and pass through a common point, they must point in
\rho
separated directions. Thus at most
\rho/\tilde{\rho}
tubes can point in directions confined to a
\rho\times\tilde{\rho}
region in
S^{2}
(this corresponds to those
\rho
tubes contained in a single
\tilde{\rho}
tube), and at most
(\tau^{\prime}/\tilde{\rho})^{\bm{\beta}}
distinct
\tilde{\rho}
tubes can contribute to the count. This implies that

\Big|\bigcup_{\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{X}}\tilde{Y}^{\prime}(\tilde{T})\Big|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\Big(\frac{\tilde{\rho}}{\tau^{\prime}}\Big)^{\bm{\beta}}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\tilde{\rho}}\Big)\sum_{\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{X}}|\tilde{Y}^{\prime}(\tilde{T})|\geq\delta^{-\bm{\zeta}/200}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\tilde{\rho}}\Big)|X|,

where the second inequality used (
8.65
) and
\bm{\beta}<\bm{\zeta}/200
. Since the set on the LHS is contained in
X
, we obtain (
8.66
), as claimed.

Next, for each prism
X\in\mathcal{X}
there exists a set
\{U\}
of essentially distinct
\rho\times\tilde{\rho}\times 1
prisms, each of which are contained in
X
, so that these sets form a partitioning cover of
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{X}^{\prime}
, and for each such
U
, the pair
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{X}^{\prime}[U],\tilde{Y}^{\prime})_{\rho}
is broad with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}1
relative to the cap of diameter
\tilde{\rho}
centered at the point
\mathrm{dir}(U)
. See Figure
14
.

[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]

Figure 14:
We find a set of
\rho\times\tilde{\rho}\times 1
prisms (blue) inside
X
(black prism), which forms a partitioning cover of
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{X}^{\prime}

(red lines). A typical pair of tubes from
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{X}^{\prime}
inside a common (blue)
\rho\times\tilde{\rho}\times 1
prism intersect at angle roughly
\tilde{\rho}
.

For each such prism
U
, define

Y(U)=\bigcup_{\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{X}^{\prime}[U]}\tilde{Y}^{\prime}(\tilde{T}).

Then a Cordoba-style
L^{2}
argument shows that for each prism
U
for which
(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{X}^{\prime}[U],\tilde{Y}^{\prime})_{\rho}
is
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{2\varepsilon_{2}}
dense (here
\tilde{Y}^{\prime}
denotes the restriction of the shading on
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{X}^{\prime}
to
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{X}^{\prime}[U]
), we have
|Y(U)|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{8\varepsilon_{2}}|U|
. Let
\mathcal{U}
denote the set of all prisms
U
for which this holds, as
X
ranges over the elements of
\mathcal{X}
. Note, however, that the prisms in
\mathcal{U}
need not be essentially distinct.

Step 13
.
Our task in this step is to unwind the various transformations and rescalings from the previous step, and to understand what the prisms
U
(and their associated shadings
Y(U)
) correspond to in the original space in which the tubes
\mathbb{T}
and prisms
\mathcal{P}
reside.

In Step 12, we fixed a choice of
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
and
W\in\mathcal{W}_{Z}
. Recall that
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}
comes from
(\mathcal{P}_{5}\langle Z\rangle)^{Z}
, and is a set of
\rho\times\rho\times 1
-tubes. We obtained a set
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{W,Z}
of
\rho\times\tilde{\rho}\times 1
prisms (this
\mathcal{U}
is different from the set
\mathcal{U}
in Step 3, the latter
\mathcal{U}
was defined to introduce
\mathcal{Z}
and only appeared within Step 3), and a shading
Y(U)
on these prisms. These prisms are contained inside
W
. Undoing the linear transformation
\phi_{Z}
, we have a set
\phi_{Z}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{W,Z})
of prisms. After pigeonholing, we may assume that these prisms are of dimensions
\tilde{a}\times\tilde{b}\times c
, for some
\tilde{a}\geq a
and
\tilde{b}\geq b
. Since the linear transformation
\phi_{Z}
distorts volume by a factor of
(a/\rho)c^{2}
, we have that
(\tilde{a}\tilde{b})=ac\tilde{\rho}
. The dimensions
\tilde{a},\tilde{b}
depend on the choice of
W
and
Z
, but after pigeonholing, we may suppose that these values are the same (up to a factor of 2) for all
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
and all
W\in\mathcal{W}_{Z}
. We claim (provided
\varepsilon_{2}
is chosen sufficiently small compared to
\varepsilon_{3}
) that either

\tilde{a}\leq\delta^{-\varepsilon_{3}}a,

(8.67)

or else Conclusion (A) of Lemma
8.3
holds (provided
\alpha
is chosen sufficiently small, depending on
\varepsilon_{3}
). The argument is identical to the argument in Step 1 of Lemma
8.2
; we refer the reader there for details.

Henceforth we shall assume that (
8.67
) holds. Abusing notation, we will re-define
\tilde{\rho}=\tilde{b}/c
; this re-definition might decrease the value of
\tilde{\rho}
by as much as
\delta^{\varepsilon_{3}}
, and (
8.66
) might be weakened to
\tilde{\rho}\geq\delta^{-\bm{\zeta}/400}\rho
. With this re-definition of
\tilde{\rho}
, we clearly have
\tilde{b}=\tilde{\rho}c
.

Let

\mathcal{Q}=\bigcup_{Z\in\mathcal{Z}}\bigcup_{W\in\mathcal{W}_{Z}}\phi_{Z}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{Z,W}).

For each
Q\in\mathcal{Q}
of the form
Q=\phi_{Z}^{-1}(U)
, define the shading
Y(Q)=\phi_{Z}^{-1}(Y(U))
and define the set
\mathcal{P}_{Q}\subset\mathcal{P}[Q]
as
\phi_{Z}^{-1}(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{X}^{\prime}[U])
, where
X
is the
\rho\times\frac{\tau^{\prime}}{\theta}\rho\times 1
-prism associated to
U
(see Step 12). Summarizing our conclusions thus far, we have the following.

•

Each
Q\in\mathcal{Q}
is a prism of dimensions
\tilde{a}\times\tilde{b}\times c
, with
\tilde{b}=\tilde{\rho}c
.

•

For each
Q\in\mathcal{Q}
, there is a set
\mathcal{P}_{Q}\subset\mathcal{P}[Q]
. Define
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}}\mathcal{P}_{Q}
. There is a shading
Y^{\prime}(P)\subset Y(P)
, so that
(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{a\times b\times c}
is a
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{2\varepsilon_{2}}
refinement of
(\mathcal{P},Y)_{a\times b\times c}
.

•

Each
Q\in\mathcal{Q}
has a shading
Y(Q)
given by

Y(Q)=\bigcup_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{Q}}Y^{\prime}(P).

We have
|Y(Q)|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{8\varepsilon_{2}}|Q|
for each
Q\in\mathcal{Q}
.

•

For each
Q\in\mathcal{Q}
and each
x\in Y(Q)
, the prisms
P\in\mathcal{P}_{Q}
with
x\in Y^{\prime}(P)
point in directions that are broad with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}1
inside a cap of radius
\tilde{b}/c
centered at
\operatorname{dir}(Q)
(these correspond to the tubes
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{X}[U]
and their associated shading, which are broad with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}1
inside the
\rho\times\tilde{\rho}\times 1
prism
U
).

•

The refinement of
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
induced by the refinement
(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{a\times b\times c}
of
(\mathcal{P},Y)_{a\times b\times c}
is
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{2\varepsilon_{2}}
dense. If we denote this refinement by
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
, then (by the definition of being an induced refinement) we have

\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}(T)=\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}}Y(Q).

The pair
(\mathcal{Q},Y)_{\tilde{a}\times\tilde{b}\times c}
has some of the desired properties from Conclusion (C) of Lemma
8.3
. Observe that for each
Q\in\mathcal{Q},

\mathrm{dir}(Q)
is defined up to uncertainty
\tilde{\rho}
. Thus after a refinement of
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
, we can find a set of
\tilde{\rho}
tubes
\mathbb{T}_{\tilde{\rho}}
and a partition
\mathcal{Q}=\bigsqcup_{T_{\tilde{\rho}}\in\mathbb{T}_{\tilde{\rho}}}\mathcal{Q}_{T_{\tilde{\rho}}}
, so that each
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{T_{\tilde{\rho}}}
is contained in
T_{\tilde{\rho}}
and satisfies
\angle(\mathrm{dir}(Q),\mathrm{dir}(T_{\tilde{\rho}}))\leq\tilde{\rho}
.

Fix a prism
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{T_{\tilde{\rho}}}
and a point
x\in Y(Q)
. The set of prisms
P\in\mathcal{P}_{Q}
with
x\in Y^{\prime}(P)
point in directions that satisfy
\angle(\mathrm{dir}(P),\mathrm{dir}(Q))\leq\tilde{\rho}
, and this set of directions is
\rho
-separated and broad with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}1
at scales
\geq\rho
inside a cap of diameter
\tilde{\rho}
centered at
\mathrm{dir}(Q)
. For each such
P
(contained in a
\rho
tube
T_{\rho}
), the set of tubes
T\in\mathbb{T}[T_{\rho}]
with
x\in Y^{\prime}(T)
are broad with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{2}}
at scales
\geq\delta
, inside a cap of diameter
\rho
centered at
\mathrm{dir}(P)
. Thus by Lemma
7.13
(broadness combines across scales) and Items (ii) and (iii) of Definition
7.1
, we have that the set of tubes
T\in\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
associated to the point
x\in Y(Q)
point in directions that are broad with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{2}}
at scales
\geq\delta
inside a cap of diameter
\tilde{\rho}
centered at
\mathrm{dir}(Q)
.

Note that even though the conclusion of Lemma
7.13
is a statement about multi-sets, here it is also true in the sense of sets. Indeed, Item (ii) of Definition
7.1
says that for each point
x\in\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{T_{\tilde{\rho}}}}Y(Q)
, we have that the sets

\{\mathrm{dir}(T):T\in\mathbb{T}[T_{\rho}]\text{ and }x\in Y^{\prime}(T)\cap Y^{\prime}(P)\}
are disjoint, as
P
ranges over the elements of
\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{T_{\tilde{\rho}}}}\mathcal{P}_{Q}
that are contained in
T_{\rho}
.
In particular, we can construct a set
\mathbb{T}_{\tilde{\rho}}
of
\tilde{\rho}
tubes that covers
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
, so that
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
is broad with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{2}}
relative to
\mathbb{T}_{\tilde{\rho}}
.

Step 14
.

We would like to show that
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
,
\mathcal{Q}
and its associated shading
Y
, and
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}
satisfy Conclusion (C) from Lemma
8.3
. First, the prisms in
\mathcal{Q}
might not be essentially distinct. This can be fixed, however, using the same argument as was employed in Step 5 from the proof of Lemma
8.2
. In brief, we merge comparable prisms from
\mathcal{Q}
into a single prism. Denote the resulting set of prisms by
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}
; these are essentially distinct prisms of dimensions comparable to
\tilde{a}\times\tilde{b}\times c
. Let
\tilde{Y}
be the shading on the prisms of
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}
obtained by taking the union of the shadings
Y(Q),\ Q\subset\tilde{P}
. We define

\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{P}}=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathcal{Q}\\
Q\subset\tilde{P}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{P}_{Q}.

Note that if
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{P}}
, then
\angle(\mathrm{dir}(P),\mathrm{dir}(\tilde{P}))\lesssim\tilde{b}/c=\tilde{\rho}
. Thus after enlarging
\tilde{a}
and
\tilde{b}
by a constant factor if needed, we can ensure that if (i):
T\cap P\neq\emptyset
, (ii):
T
exists
P
through its long ends, and (iii):
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{P}}
, then
T\cap\tilde{P}\neq\emptyset
and
T
exists
\tilde{P}
through its long ends.
At this point, the pair
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
and
(\tilde{\mathcal{P}},\tilde{Y})_{\tilde{a}\times\tilde{b}\times c}
satisfy some of the requirements of Conclusion (C) from Lemma
8.3
. The situation matches the setup at the end of Step 5 in the proof of Lemma
8.2
.

We now proceed with the same argument that was used in Steps 6 – 8 from the proof of Lemma
8.2
. We conclude that either Conclusion (A) of Lemma
8.3
holds (this is the same as Conclusion (A) of Lemma
8.2
), or else there is a set
\mathbb{T}_{\tilde{\rho}}
and a further refinement of
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
and
(\tilde{P},\tilde{Y})_{\tilde{a}\times\tilde{b}\times c}
that satisfies Items (i), (ii), and (iii) of Conclusion (C) from Lemma
8.3
(Items (i), (ii), and (iii) of Conclusion (C) from Lemma
8.2
). Note that Item (iv) Conclusion (C) is also satisfied, since
\tilde{\rho}\geq\delta^{-\bm{\zeta}/400}\rho
. We conclude that Conclusion (C) from Lemma
8.3
holds.
∎

9
A refined induction-on-scales argument

The goal in this section is to show that if
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
is a set of
\delta
-tubes, then either
\bigcup_{\mathbb{T}}Y(T)
has larger volume than one would expect from the estimate (
1.3
) from Assertion
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega)
, or else there exists a scale
\delta<\!\!<\rho<\!\!<1
and a set of
\rho
tubes that factors
\mathbb{T}
above and below with respect to the Katz-Tao Convex Wolff Axioms and Frostman Slab Wolff Axioms. The precise statement is as follows.

Proposition 9.1
.

Let
\omega,\zeta>0
and
\sigma\in(0,2/3]
, and suppose that
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega)
is true. Then there exists

\alpha,\eta,\kappa>0

so that the following holds for all
\delta>0
.
Let
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
be
\delta^{\eta}
dense, and suppose that
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})\leq\delta^{-\eta}
and
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T})\leq\delta^{-\eta}
. Then at least one of the following must hold.

(A)

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|\geq\kappa\delta^{\omega-\alpha}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|\big((\#\mathbb{T})|T|^{1/2}\big)^{-\sigma}.

(9.1)

(B)

There exists a refinement
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime},Y^{\prime})_{\delta}
of
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
that is
\delta^{\zeta}
dense, a number
\rho\in[\delta^{1-\omega/100},\delta^{\omega/100}]
, and a set
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
that factors
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
above and below with respect to both the Katz-Tao Convex Wolff Axioms and the Frostman Slab Wolff Axioms, both with error
\leq\delta^{-\zeta}
.

Proof.

Step 1.

Let
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3}
be small numbers to be chosen below. We will select
\varepsilon_{1}
very small compared to
\varepsilon_{2}
and
\varepsilon_{2}
very small compared to
\varepsilon_{3}
. These numbers depend on
\omega,\sigma,
and
\zeta
. We will select
\eta
and
\alpha
very small compared to
\varepsilon_{1}
.

Apply Proposition
7.5
(two scale grains decomposition) to
(\mathbb{T},Y)
with
\varepsilon_{1}
in place of
\zeta
, and let
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{1}(\omega,\sigma,\varepsilon_{1})
be the output of that proposition. If Conclusion (A) of Proposition
7.5
holds, then (
9.1
) is true (provided we select
\alpha\leq\alpha_{1}
), and we are done.

Next, suppose Conclusion (B) of Proposition
7.5
holds. Let
\rho,c
,
(\mathbb{T}_{1},Y_{1})_{\delta},

\mathbb{T}_{\rho},
and
(\mathcal{G},Y)_{a\times\rho c\times c}
be the output from Proposition
7.5
, Conclusion (B).

Apply Proposition
4.6
(factoring convex sets) to
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
. We obtain a
\approx_{\rho}1
refinement of
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
, which in turn induces a
\approx_{\rho}1
refinement of
(\mathbb{T}_{1},Y_{1})_{\delta}
(abusing notation, we will continue to refer to these objects as
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
and
(\mathbb{T}_{1},Y_{1})_{\delta}
) and a collection
\mathcal{Z}
of congruent convex sets that factors
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
from above with respect to the Katz-Tao Convex Wolff Axioms and from below with respect to the Frostman Convex Wolff Axioms, both with error
\lessapprox_{\rho}1
. Furthermore,

\#\mathbb{T}_{\rho}[Z]\gtrapprox_{\rho}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\rho})|Z||T_{\rho}|^{-1}\quad\textrm{for each}\ Z\in\mathcal{Z}.

(9.2)

If
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\rho})\leq\delta^{-\zeta},
then provided we select
\varepsilon_{1}\leq\zeta/2
, we have that
(\mathbb{T}_{1},Y_{1})
and
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
satisfy Conclusion (B) of Proposition
9.1
, and we are done. Indeed; by Remark
4.3
(A) we have
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\rho})\lesssim C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T}_{1})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\eta_{1}-\varepsilon_{1}}\leq\delta^{-\zeta}
, while by Remark
4.3
(B) we have
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{1}^{T_{\rho}})\lesssim C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})\leq\delta^{-\eta}
for each
T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
.

Step 2.

We now consider the case where
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\rho})>\delta^{-\zeta}
, and hence

\#\mathbb{T}_{\rho}[Z]\gtrsim\delta^{-\zeta}\frac{|Z|}{|T_{\rho}|}\quad\textrm{for each}\ Z\in\mathcal{Z}.

(9.3)

Our goal is to show that Conclusion (A) of Proposition
9.1
holds, provided
\alpha>0
is chosen appropriately.

First, we claim that either Conclusion (A) of Proposition
9.1
holds, or else the prisms in
\mathcal{Z}
are almost tubes. Indeed, let
t\times\theta\times 2
be the dimensions of the prisms in
\mathcal{Z}
. If
\varepsilon_{1}
is chosen sufficiently small depending on
\varepsilon_{2},\omega,
and
\sigma
, then by applying Proposition
5.2
with
\varepsilon_{2}
in place of
\varepsilon
, we have

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}}Y_{1}(T)\Big|\gtrsim\delta^{\omega+\varepsilon_{2}}\Big(\frac{\theta}{t}\Big)^{\omega}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|\big((\#\mathbb{T})|T|^{1/2}\big)^{-\sigma},

(9.4)

where the implicit constant depends on
\varepsilon_{2}
. In particular, we may suppose that

t\geq\delta^{\varepsilon_{3}}\theta,

(9.5)

or else Conclusion (A) of Proposition
9.1
holds, provided
\varepsilon_{2}\leq\varepsilon_{3}\omega/2
and
\alpha\leq\varepsilon_{3}\omega/2
. Replace each
t\times\theta\times 2
prism
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
with its coaxial
\theta
-tube. After dyadic pigeonholing and replacing
(\mathbb{T}_{1},Y_{1})_{\delta}
and
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
with a
\approx_{\delta}1
refinement, we can find a balanced cover
\mathbb{T}_{\theta}
of
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
that factors
\mathbb{T}_{\theta}
from below with respect to the Frostman Convex Wolff Axioms and from above with respect to the Katz-Tao Wolff Axioms, both with error
\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\varepsilon_{3}}
.

(
9.3
) implies that for each
T_{\theta}\in\mathbb{T}_{\theta}
we have
\#\mathbb{T}_{\rho}[T_{\theta}]\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\varepsilon_{3}-\zeta}(\theta/\rho)^{2}
, and hence

\#\mathbb{T}_{\rho}\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\varepsilon_{3}-\zeta}(\theta/\rho)^{2}(\#\mathbb{T}_{\theta}).

(9.6)

Step 3.

For each
T_{\theta}\in\mathbb{T}_{\theta}
, define

\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}=\bigcup_{T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}[T_{\theta}]}\mathcal{G}_{T_{\rho}}.

We claim that either Conclusion (A) holds (for a suitably chosen value of
\alpha
), or else there is a
\approx_{\delta}1
refinement of
(\mathcal{G},Y)_{a\times\rho c\times c}
so that the following holds:
a\in[\delta,\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{1}}]
and for each
T_{\theta}\in\mathbb{T}_{\theta}
and each
G\in\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}
, the set of grains
G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}
with
Y(G)\cap Y(G^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
is contained in a prism of dimensions comparable to
\frac{\theta\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{2}}}{\rho}\times\theta c\times c
(compare this with the dimensions of
G
, which are
a\times\rho c\times c
). The argument is identical to the argument in Steps 1 and 2 from Lemma
8.3
; we refer the reader to those Steps for details.

We shall suppose henceforth that for each
T_{\theta}\in\mathbb{T}_{\theta}
and each
G\in\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}
, the set of grains
G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}
with
Y(G)\cap Y(G^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
is contained in a prism of dimensions comparable to
\frac{\theta\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{2}}}{\rho}\times\theta c\times c
.

Step 4.

By dyadic pigeonholing we can find a number
\mu_{\operatorname{fine}}
and a
\approx_{\delta}1
refinement of
(\mathbb{T}_{1},Y_{1})_{\delta}
and
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
so that for each
T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
and each
x\in\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}[T_{\rho}]}Y_{1}(T)
, we have

\#\big((\mathbb{T}_{1}[T_{\rho}])_{Y_{1}}(x)\big)\sim\mu_{\operatorname{fine}}.

We can choose these refinements so it continues to be the case that
(\mathbb{T}_{1}^{T_{\rho}},Y_{1}^{T_{\rho}})_{\delta/\rho}
is
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\varepsilon_{1}}
dense and
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T}_{1}^{T_{\rho}})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\varepsilon_{1}}
for each
T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
(recall that we still have
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{1}^{T_{\rho}})\leq\delta^{-\eta}
).

If
\varepsilon_{1}
is chosen sufficiently small depending on
\varepsilon_{2}
, then we can apply the estimate
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega)
to conclude that for each
T_{\rho}\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
, we have

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}[T_{\rho}]}Y_{1}(T)\Big|\gtrsim\big(\frac{\delta}{\rho}\big)^{\omega+\varepsilon_{2}}(\#\mathbb{T}_{1}[T_{\rho}])|T|\Big((\#\mathbb{T}_{1}[T_{\rho}])\big(\frac{|T|}{|T_{\rho}|}\big)^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma},

where the implicit constant depends on
\varepsilon_{2}
, and hence by (
9.6
),

\mu_{\operatorname{fine}}\lesssim\Big(\frac{\delta}{\rho}\Big)^{-\omega-\varepsilon_{2}}\Big(\frac{\#\mathbb{T}_{1}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{\rho}}\frac{\delta}{\rho}\Big)^{\sigma}\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{3}+\sigma\zeta}\Big(\frac{\delta}{\rho}\Big)^{-\omega}\Big(\frac{\#\mathbb{T}_{1}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{\theta}}\frac{\delta\rho}{\theta^{2}}\Big)^{\sigma}.

(9.7)

Step 5.

In previous applications of induction on scale, the estimate (
9.7
) would be paired with a multiplicity estimate on the tubes in
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
. Our innovation, however, is to pair the estimate (
9.7
) with a multiplicity estimate on
\mathcal{G}
.

After refining the pair
(\mathcal{G},Y)_{a\times\rho c\times c}
by a
\approx_{\delta}1
factor (this in turn refines
(\mathbb{T}_{1},Y_{1})_{\delta}
by a similar quantity), we can find a number
\mu_{\operatorname{medium}}
so that for each
T_{\theta}\in\mathbb{T}_{\theta}
and each
x\in\bigcup_{G\in\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}}Y(G)
, we have

\#\{G\in\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}\colon x\in Y(G)\}\sim\mu_{\operatorname{medium}}.

Our task is to estimate
\mu_{\operatorname{medium}}
. Recalling the conclusion of Step 3, we can cover
T_{\theta}
by rectangular prisms
P
of dimensions comparable to
\frac{\theta\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{2}}}{\rho}\times\theta c\times c
, so that every pair of grains
G,G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}
with
Y(G)\cap Y(G^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
are contained in a common prism. Let
\mathcal{P}
denote this set of prisms; then for each
P\in\mathcal{P}
,
\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}^{P}
is a set of prisms, each of which has dimensions roughly
\frac{\rho}{\theta}\times\frac{\rho}{\theta}\times 1
(more precisely, each prism in
\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}^{P}
has dimensions comparable to
s\times t\times 1
, where
s,t\in[\delta^{\varepsilon_{2}}\frac{\rho}{\theta},\frac{\rho}{\theta}]
; this additional
\delta^{\varepsilon_{2}}
factor will be harmless). After pigeonholing, we may assume that the lengths
s
and
t
are the same for every
T_{\theta}\in\mathbb{T}_{\theta}
and every
P\in\mathcal{P}
.

We have
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}^{P})\lesssim\delta^{-\varepsilon_{2}}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathcal{G})\leq\delta^{-\varepsilon_{1}-\varepsilon_{2}}
, while

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}^{P})\leq C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}^{P})\leq C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}^{P})(\rho/\theta)^{-2}(\#\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}^{P})^{-1}\leq\delta^{-\varepsilon_{1}-2\varepsilon_{2}}(\rho/\theta)^{-2}(\#\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}^{P})^{-1}.

If
\varepsilon_{2}
is selected sufficiently small depending on
\varepsilon_{3}
,
\omega
, and
\sigma
, then we can apply Assertion
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega)
to conclude that

\begin{split}\Big|\bigcup_{G^{P}\in\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}^{P}}Y^{P}(G^{P})\Big|&\geq\big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\big)^{\omega+\varepsilon_{3}}\delta^{5\varepsilon_{2}}(\#\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}^{P})|G^{P}|\Big(\big[(\rho\theta)^{-2}(\#\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}^{P})^{-1}\big]\big[\#\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}^{P}\big]\big[|G^{P}|^{1/2}]\Big)^{-\sigma}\\
&\gtrapprox_{\delta}\big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\big)^{\omega+\varepsilon_{3}}\delta^{5\varepsilon_{2}}(\#\mathcal{G}_{T_{\theta}}^{P})|G^{P}|\big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\big)^{\sigma},\end{split}

and thus

\mu_{\operatorname{medium}}\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{3}}\big(\frac{\rho}{\theta}\big)^{-\omega-\sigma}.

(9.8)

Step 6.

At this point, we have estimated the quantities
\mu_{\operatorname{fine}}
and
\mu_{\operatorname{medium}}
. The former allows us to control the number of
\delta
tubes that contribute to (a specific point in) a grain, while the latter allows us to control the number of grains that contribute to (a specific point in) a
\theta
tube.

It remains to place a dense shading on
\mathbb{T}_{\theta}
and obtain a corresponding multiplicity estimate for the number of
\theta
tubes that contribute to (a specific point in)
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. After dyadic pigeonholing, we can refine
(\mathbb{T}_{1},Y_{1})_{\delta}
and
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
so that for each
T_{\theta}\in\mathbb{T}_{\theta}
and each
x\in\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}[T_{\theta}]}Y_{1}(T)
, we have that
|B(x,\theta)\cap\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}[T_{\theta}]}Y(T)|
has roughly the same volume. Let
Y(T_{\theta})=T_{\theta}\cap N_{\theta}\big(\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}[T_{\theta}]}Y_{1}(T)\big);
then
(\mathbb{T}_{\theta},Y)_{\theta}
is
\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\varepsilon_{1}}
dense. After further pigeonholing we can find a number
\mu_{\operatorname{coarse}}
so that

\#(\mathbb{T}_{\theta})_{Y}(x)\sim\mu_{\operatorname{coarse}}\quad\textrm{for each}\ x\in\bigcup_{T_{\theta}\in\mathbb{T}_{\theta}}Y(T_{\theta}).

By Remark
4.3
(A), we have
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\theta})\lesssim C_{\scriptscriptstyle{F\textrm{-}SW}}(\mathbb{T})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\varepsilon_{1}}
, and
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\theta})\lesssim\delta^{-\varepsilon_{3}}C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathcal{Z})\lessapprox_{\delta}\delta^{-\varepsilon_{3}}
. Thus if
\varepsilon_{1}
is chosen sufficiently small compared to
\varepsilon_{2}
, then we can apply
\mathcal{E}(\sigma,\omega)
to conclude that

\begin{split}\Big|\bigcup_{T_{\theta}\in\mathbb{T}_{\theta}}Y(T_{\theta})\Big|&\gtrsim\theta^{\omega+\varepsilon_{2}+\varepsilon_{3}}(\#\mathbb{T}_{\theta})|T_{\theta}|\Big((\#\mathbb{T}_{\theta})|T_{\theta}|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma},\end{split}

and hence

\mu_{\operatorname{coarse}}\lesssim\theta^{-\omega-\varepsilon_{2}-\varepsilon_{3}}\Big((\#\mathbb{T}_{\theta})\theta\Big)^{\sigma}.

(9.9)

Combining (
9.7
), (
9.8
), and (
9.9
), we conclude that for each
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
we have

\begin{split}\#\{T\in\mathbb{T}_{1}\colon x\in Y_{1}(T)\}&\leq\mu_{\operatorname{fine}}\ \mu_{\operatorname{medium}}\ \mu_{\operatorname{coarse}}\\
&\lessapprox\delta^{-4\varepsilon_{3}+\sigma\zeta}\delta^{-\omega}\Big((\#\mathbb{T})\delta\Big)^{\sigma}.\end{split}

(9.10)

We conclude that

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\omega+4\varepsilon_{3}-\sigma\zeta}(\#\mathbb{T}_{1})|T|\Big((\#\mathbb{T})|T|^{1/2}\Big)^{-\sigma}.

Since
\#\mathbb{T}_{1}\gtrapprox_{\delta}\delta^{\varepsilon_{1}}(\#\mathbb{T})
, we have that Conclusion (A) holds, provided we select
\varepsilon_{3}<\sigma\zeta/10
and
\alpha<\sigma\zeta/10
.
∎

10
Sticky Kakeya for tubes satisfying the Katz-Tao Convex Wolff Axioms at every Scale

In Section
6
, we recalled a version of the Sticky Kakeya Theorem that was proved in
[
26
]
; this is Theorem
6.2
. Theorem
6.2
applies to families of tubes that satisfy the Frostman Convex Wolff Axioms at every scale, in the sense of Definition
6.1
. In this section, we will prove an analogue of Theorem
6.2
for sets of tubes that satisfy the
Katz-Tao
Convex Wolff Axioms at every scale.

Definition 10.1
.

Let
K\geq 1,\delta>0
. We say a set
\mathbb{T}
of essentially distinct
\delta
-tubes satisfies the
Katz-Tao Wolff Axioms at every scale with error
K
if for every
\rho_{0}\in[\delta,1]
, there exists
\rho\in[\rho_{0},K\rho_{0})
and a set of
\rho
-tubes
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
that satisfies the following properties.

(i)

\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
is a
K
-balanced partitioning cover of
\mathbb{T}
.

(ii)

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{\rho})\leq K
.

Theorem 10.2
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\eta,\kappa>0
so that the following holds for all
\delta>0
. Let
\mathbb{T}
be a set of
\delta
-tubes that satisfy the Katz-Tao Convex Wolff Axioms at every scale with error
\delta^{-\eta}
, and let
Y(T)
be a
\delta^{\eta}
dense shading. Then

\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|\geq\kappa\delta^{\varepsilon}(\#\mathbb{T})|T|.

(10.1)

In the next section, we will combine Theorem
10.2
with Proposition
9.1
to prove Proposition
1.7
. Theorem
10.2
is proved by combining Theorem
6.2
with the following Nikishin-Stein-Pisier Factorization type result.

Proposition 10.3
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
. Then there exists
K_{\varepsilon},\eta>0
so that the following holds for all
\delta>0
. Let
\mathbb{T}
be a non-empty set of
\delta
tubes inside the unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
that satisfy the Katz-Tao Convex Wolff axioms at every scale with error
\delta^{-\eta}
. Then there exist rigid transformations
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N},

N\leq K_{\varepsilon}(\#\mathbb{T})^{-1}|T|^{-1}
so that each set
A_{i}(\mathbb{T})
is contained inside
B(0,2)
, and
\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}A_{i}(\mathbb{T})
contains a subset of essentially distinct tubes that satisfies the Frostman Convex Wolff Axioms at every scale with error
K_{\varepsilon}\delta^{-\varepsilon}
.

Proof of Theorem
10.2
using Proposition
10.3
.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
and let
\eta=\eta(\varepsilon)>0
be a small quantity to be determined below. Let
(\mathbb{T},Y)_{\delta}
be
\delta^{\eta}
dense, and suppose that
\mathbb{T}
satisfies the Katz-Tao Wolff Axioms at every scale with error
\delta^{-\eta}
. After a harmless refinement we may suppose
|Y(T)|\geq\delta^{2\eta}|T|
for each
T\in\mathbb{T}
.

Apply Proposition
10.3
with a small value
\varepsilon_{1}
in place of
\varepsilon
. We may do this, provided
\eta
is selected sufficiently small depending on
\varepsilon_{1}
. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}A_{i}(\mathbb{T})
be the output from Proposition
10.3
. Note that each
\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}
is of the form
\tilde{T}=A_{i}(T)
for some index
i
and some
T\in\mathbb{T}
, and hence we can define the shading
\tilde{Y}(\tilde{T})=A_{i}(Y(T))
; we have
|\tilde{Y}(\tilde{T})|=|Y(T)|\geq\delta^{2\eta}|T|
, and hence
(\tilde{T},\tilde{Y})_{\delta}
is
\delta^{2\eta}
dense.

If
\varepsilon_{1}
and
\eta
are chosen sufficiently small depending on
\varepsilon
, then we can apply Theorem
6.2
to conclude that

N\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}Y(T)\Big|=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big|\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}A_{i}(Y(T))\Big|\geq\Big|\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{T}}A_{i}(Y(T))\Big|\geq\Big|\bigcup_{\tilde{T}\in\tilde{\mathbb{T}}}\tilde{Y}(\tilde{T})\Big|\geq\kappa_{\varepsilon}\delta^{\varepsilon}.

Re-arranging and noting that
N\leq K_{\varepsilon}(\#\mathbb{T})^{-1}|T|^{-1}
, we obtain (
10.1
), with
\kappa=\kappa_{\varepsilon}K_{\varepsilon}^{-1}
.
∎

It remains to prove Proposition
10.3
. We will do so below.

10.1
Nikishin-Stein-Pisier Factorization and the Convex Wolff Axioms

We begin with a single-scale version of Proposition
10.3
. We first need the following definition.

Definition 10.4
.

We say that a set
\mathbb{T}
of
\delta
tubes is regular with granularity
\tau\in[\delta,1]
if for every scale
\rho\in[\delta,1]
of the form
\rho=\delta\tau^{-\ell}
,
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that
\mathbb{T}
has a balanced partitioning cover by
\rho
tubes.

Definition 10.5
.

For
\rho>0
, we define
\mathfrak{A}_{\rho}
to be the set of rigid transformations
A\colon\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
that satisfy
|Ax-x|\leq\rho
for all
x\in B(0,1)
.

Lemma 10.6
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\eta>0
and
K_{\varepsilon}\geq 1
so that the following holds for all
0<\delta\leq\rho\leq 1
. Let
K,M\geq 1
and let
\mathbb{T}_{1},\ldots,\mathbb{T}_{K}
be sets of
\delta
tubes in
B(0,1)\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
, each of cardinality at most
M
. Suppose that the tubes in each set
\mathbb{T}_{j}
are regular with granularity
\delta^{\eta}
, and furthermore each set
\mathbb{T}_{j}
is contained in a
\rho
tube.

Then there exists a set of rigid transformations
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathfrak{A}_{\rho}
with
\#\mathcal{A}=\big\lceil\frac{\rho^{2}}{M\delta^{2}}\big\rceil
, so that

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}\Big(\bigsqcup_{A\in\mathcal{A}}A(\mathbb{T}_{j})\Big)\leq K_{\varepsilon}\delta^{-\varepsilon}(\log(2+K))C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{j}),\quad j=1,\ldots,K.

(10.2)

Remark 10.7
.

Note that for distinct
A,A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
, the sets
A(\mathbb{T}_{j})
and
A^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{j})
might contain common tubes, and thus the disjoint union on the LHS of (
10.2
) should be interpreted as a multiset. By Remark
4.2
(D), the LHS of (
10.2
) is well-defined.

Proof.

Step 1.

Define
\tilde{\delta}=\delta/\rho
. First, we may suppose that
M<\tilde{\delta}^{-2}
, or else we can define
\mathcal{A}=\{I\}
(here
I\colon\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
is the identity map) and we are done. Similarly, we may suppose
\rho\geq\delta^{1-\varepsilon/2}
, or else we can define
\mathcal{A}
to be
\big\lceil\frac{\rho^{2}}{M\delta^{2}}\big\rceil
infinitesimally perturbed copies of
I
, and (
10.2
) follows from the fact that

C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}\Big(\bigsqcup_{A\in\mathcal{A}}A(\mathbb{T}_{j})\Big)\leq(\#\mathcal{A})C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T}_{j}).

Fix an index
j\in[1,\ldots,K]
and let
\mathbb{T}=\mathbb{T}_{j}
. By hypothesis, all of the tubes in
\mathbb{T}
are contained in a common
\rho
tube, which we will denote by
T_{\rho}
. Fix numbers
\delta\leq a\leq b\leq 2\rho
, with both
a
and
b
of the form
\delta^{\ell\eta}
. Let
\nu\geq 1
be a power of 2. By hypothesis,
\mathbb{T}
has a balanced partitioning cover
\mathbb{T}_{a}
.

Let
\mathcal{W}_{\nu}
be a maximal set of essentially distinct
a\times b\times 2
prisms, each of which satisfy
\#\mathbb{T}[W]\in\big[\nu\frac{\#\mathbb{T}\phantom{{}_{a}}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{a}},\ 2\nu\frac{\#\mathbb{T}\phantom{{}_{a}}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{a}}\big)
. Note that each
W\in\mathcal{W}_{\nu}
is contained in
N_{2\rho}(T_{\rho})
. Observe that if
W
is an
a\times b\times 2
prism and
T\in\mathbb{T}[T_{a}]
with
T\subset W
, then
T_{a}\subset 2W
. In particular, since
\mathbb{T}_{a}
is a balanced partitioning cover of
\mathbb{T}
, we have

\#\mathbb{T}_{a}[2W]\geq\nu/2\quad\textrm{for each}\ W\in\mathcal{W}_{\nu}.

Each tube
T_{a}\in\mathbb{T}_{a}
is contained in
\lesssim\frac{b}{a}
essentially distinct
2a\times 2b\times 4
prisms. Thus by double-counting we have

\#\mathcal{W}_{\nu}\lesssim(\#\mathbb{T}_{a})\frac{b}{a}\nu^{-1}.

(10.3)

The above estimate is useful when
\nu
is not too large. When
\nu\frac{\#\mathbb{T}\phantom{{}_{a}}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{a}}\geq C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})(ab)|T|^{-1}
, then
\mathcal{W}_{\nu}=\emptyset
.

Step 2.

We say two rigid motions
A,A^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{A}_{\rho}
are
\delta
-separated if there exists a point
x\in B(0,1)
with
|A(x)-A^{\prime}(x)|\geq\delta
. Let
\mathfrak{A}_{\rho}^{\delta}
be a maximal
\delta
-separated subset of
\mathfrak{A}_{\rho}
; we have
\#\mathfrak{A}^{\delta}_{\rho}\sim\tilde{\delta}^{-6}=\delta^{6}/\rho^{6}
.

Let

N=2\Big\lceil\frac{\rho^{2}}{M\delta^{2}}\Big\rceil,

(10.4)

and let
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
be chosen uniformly and independently at random from
\mathfrak{A}_{\rho}^{\delta}
. We have

\mathbb{P}\big(\#\{A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}\}\geq N/2\big)\geq 3/4,

(10.5)

where
\#\{A_{1},\dots,A_{N}\}
denotes the number of distinct rigid motions in the set
\{A_{1},\dots,A_{N}\}
.

Fix a
a\times b\times 2
prism
W_{0}
. We would like to estimate the probability that

\#\Big(\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{N}A_{i}(\mathbb{T})\Big)[W_{0}]\geq K_{\varepsilon}\delta^{-\varepsilon/2}(\log(2+K))C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})|W_{0}||T|^{-1},

(10.6)

i.e. we would like to estimate the probability that

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\#\mathbb{T}[(A_{i}^{-1}(W_{0}))]\geq K_{\varepsilon}\delta^{-\varepsilon/2}(\log(2+K))C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})|W_{0}||T|^{-1}.

(10.7)

Note that if (
10.7
) occurs, then by pigeonholing, there must be some dyadic
\nu
so that

\#\big\{(W,i)\in(\mathcal{W}_{\nu}\times\{1,\ldots,N\})\colon A_{i}(W)\ \textrm{is contained in }\ 10W_{0}\big\}\geq Z_{\nu},

(10.8)

where

Z_{\nu}=\big(4\log(1/\delta)\big)^{-1}K_{\varepsilon}\delta^{-\varepsilon/2}(\log(2+K))C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})|W_{0}||T|^{-1}\Big(\sup_{W\in\mathcal{W}_{\nu}}\#\mathbb{T}[W]\Big)^{-1}.

(10.9)

We may suppose that

\sup_{W\in\mathcal{W}_{\nu}}\#\mathbb{T}[W]\sim\nu\frac{\#\mathbb{T}}{\#\mathbb{T}_{a}}\leq C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})(ab)|T|^{-1},

(10.10)

since otherwise
\mathcal{W}_{\nu}=\emptyset
, and thus it is impossible for either of Inequality (
10.8
) or (
10.7
) to be true. Since
|W_{0}|=2ab
, we can use (
10.10
) to bound
Z_{\nu}
. We conclude that

Z_{\nu}\geq\big(2\log(1/\delta)\big)^{-1}K_{\varepsilon}\delta^{-\varepsilon/2}(\log(2+K)).

We will choose the constant
K_{\varepsilon}
sufficiently large so that
Z_{\nu}\geq 2
, and in particular

Z_{\nu}-1\sim Z_{\nu}.

(10.11)

This will be relevant in Step 3 when we apply Chernoff’s inequality.

Step 3.

We will estimate the probability that (
10.8
) occurs for a fixed choice of
\nu
and
W_{0}
. For two
a\times b\times 2
prisms
W,W_{0}
, both of which are contained inside
N_{2\rho}(T_{\rho})
we have that the number of
A\in\mathfrak{A}_{\rho}^{\delta}
for which
A(W)
is comparable to
W_{0}
(or equivalently,
A^{-1}(W_{0})
is comparable to
W
)
is
\lesssim\delta^{-6}a^{2}b^{2}\rho\min\{a/b,\rho\}.
We will write this as
\tilde{\delta}^{-6}\tilde{a}^{2}\tilde{b}^{2}\min\{\tilde{a}/(\tilde{b}\rho),1\}\leq\tilde{\delta}^{-6}\tilde{a}^{2}\tilde{b}^{2}
, where we define
\tilde{a}=a/\rho
and
\tilde{b}=b/\rho
.

The reason for this numerology is as follows. Without loss of generality, assume
W_{0}=[0,a]\times[0,b]\times[0,2]
. Then a rigid motion
A
is determined by
A(v_{i})
with
v_{0}=(0,0,0),v_{1}=(0,1,0),v_{2}=(0,0,1)
. Since
A\in\mathfrak{A}_{\rho}^{\delta}
, the number of
\delta
-separated choice for
A(v_{0})
is
\leq\frac{|W\cap B(0,\rho)|}{\delta^{3}}\sim\frac{ab\rho}{\delta^{3}}
. Once
A(v_{0})
is fixed, the number of
\delta
-separated choices for
A(v_{2})
is
\leq\frac{ab}{\delta^{2}}
. Once
A(v_{0}),A(v_{2})
are fixed, the number of
\delta
-separated choices for
A(v_{1})
is
\leq\min\{\frac{a}{b},\rho\}\delta^{-1}
.

Thus if we define
X_{i}
to be the event that there exists
W\in\mathcal{W}_{\nu}
such that
A_{i}(W)
is comparable to
W_{0}
, then

\mathbb{P}(X_{i})\lesssim\tilde{\delta}^{-6}\tilde{a}^{2}\tilde{b}^{2}\cdot\#\mathfrak{A}_{\rho}^{\delta}\cdot\#\mathcal{W}_{\nu}\lesssim\tilde{a}^{2}\tilde{b}^{2}(\#\mathcal{W}_{\nu}).

(10.12)

Since the prisms in
\mathcal{W}_{\nu}
are essentially distinct, there can exist at most
O(1)

W\in\mathcal{W}_{\nu}
such that
A_{i}(W)
is comparable to
W_{0}
. Thus by linearity of expectation, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}&\Big(\#\{(W,i)\in(\mathcal{W}_{\nu}\times\{1,\ldots,N\})\colon A_{i}(W)\ \textrm{is comparable to}\ W_{0}\}\Big)\\
&=\mathbb{E}(X_{1}+\ldots+X_{N})\\
&\lesssim N\tilde{a}^{2}\tilde{b}^{2}(\#\mathcal{W}_{\nu})\\
&\lesssim 2\Big\lceil\frac{\rho^{2}}{M\delta^{2}}\Big\rceil\tilde{a}^{2}\tilde{b}^{2}(\#\mathcal{W}_{\nu})\end{split}

On the third line we used (
10.12
); on the fourth line we used (
10.4
) and (
10.3
).

Define
X=X_{1}+\ldots+X_{N}
. Recalling (
10.9
) and (
10.11
), we have

\begin{split}\gamma:=\frac{Z_{\nu}}{\mathbb{E}(X)}&\gtrsim\log(1/\delta)^{-1}K_{\varepsilon}\delta^{-\varepsilon/2}(\log(2+K))C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})|W_{0}||T|^{-1}\Big(2\Big\lceil\frac{\rho^{2}}{M\delta^{2}}\Big\rceil\tilde{a}^{2}\tilde{b}^{2}\sup_{W\in\mathcal{W}_{\nu}}\#\mathbb{T}[W]\cdot(\#\mathcal{W}_{\nu})\Big)^{-1}\\
&\gtrsim\log(1/\delta)^{-1}K_{\varepsilon}\delta^{-\varepsilon/2}(\log(2+K))C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})|W_{0}||T|^{-1}\Big(2\Big\lceil\frac{\rho^{2}}{M\delta^{2}}\Big\rceil\tilde{a}^{2}\tilde{b}^{2}\frac{b}{a}(\#\mathbb{T})\Big)^{-1}\\
&\gtrsim\log(1/\delta)^{-1}K_{\varepsilon}\delta^{-\varepsilon/2}(\log(2+K))\end{split}

On the second line we used (
10.3
) and the inequality
\#\mathbb{T}[W]\sim\nu\frac{\#\mathbb{T}}{\mathbb{T}_{a}}
; on the third line we used the fact that
|T|\sim\delta^{2}
and the fact that
\tilde{a}=a/\rho
; and on the final line we used the fact that
C_{\scriptscriptstyle{KT\textrm{-}CW}}(\mathbb{T})\geq 1
,
\tilde{b}^{2}\leq 4
,
|W_{0}|=2ab
, and
\#\mathbb{T}\leq M
.

Hence we can apply the multiplicative Chernoff’s inequality to conclude that

\mathbb{P}(X\geq Z_{\nu})\leq\Big(\frac{e^{\gamma-1}}{\gamma^{\gamma}}\Big)^{\mathbb{E}(X)}\lesssim e^{-\gamma\mathbb{E}(X)}=e^{-Z_{\nu}}\lesssim(2+K)^{-(\log 1/\delta)^{-1}K_{\varepsilon}\delta^{-\varepsilon/2}}\lesssim K^{-1}\exp[-K_{\varepsilon}\delta^{-\varepsilon/3}].

For the second inequality, we used the fact that
K_{\varepsilon}
is sufficiently large,
\frac{e^{\gamma-1}}{\gamma^{\gamma}}\leq e^{-\gamma}
for all
\delta>0
and
K\geq 1
.

Step 4.

There are
\log(1/\delta)
choices of
\nu
; at most
a^{-3}b^{-1}\leq\delta^{-4}
essentially distinct prisms
W_{0}
; and
\eta^{-2}
choices of numbers
(a,b)
